\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y_{k},h_{k}}(z)^{2}q_{b}(z)dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{NA\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}L^{*}}{r},

where we used
h_{k}\leq A
and Lemma
D.1
. For
r\to\infty
, this expression tends to zero and therefore (
F.10
) holds true by Lemma
F.5
. Next, we have to consider an additional
\sup_{B\subset\mathcal{T}_{c}:\#B\leq N}
instead of fixed
N
, but for the statement of the theorem, it suffices to consider
B\subset\mathcal{T}_{c}=\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{c}\cap\mathbb{Q}^{2}
. Suppose that for some
n_{0}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{B\subset\mathcal{T}_{c}:\#B\leq n_{0}}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R}^{\#B})}\left|\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[f((Z_{T}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B})\right]\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 156.49014pt\left.-\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[f((Z_{b}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B})\right]\right|

does not tend to zero for
T\to\infty
. Then there exists a sequence
(T_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
T_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle n\to\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}\infty
together with
(B_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathcal{T}_{c}
,
\#B_{n}\leq n_{0}
, and
\epsilon>0
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R}^{\#B_{n}})}\left|\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[f((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B_{n}})\right]\right.\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\left.-\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[f((Z_{b}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B_{n}})\right]\right|>\epsilon.\end{split}

(F.11)

We will show that this is not possible. First of all, there exists an integer
\tilde{n}_{0}\leq n_{0}
and a subsequence
(n_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
\#B_{n_{k}}=\tilde{n}_{0}
, as there are only finitely many integers
\leq n_{0}
. Furthermore, as
\mathcal{T}_{c}=\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{c}\cap\mathbb{Q}^{2}
and
\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{c}^{\tilde{n}_{0}}
is compact, there again exists a subsubsequence
(n_{k_{l}})_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
such that the vector of locations
((y,h))_{(y,h)\in B_{n_{k_{l}}}}
converges to a vector

\left((y_{0}^{1},h_{0}^{1}),\dots,(y_{0}^{\tilde{n}_{0}},h_{0}^{\tilde{n}_{0}})\right)=:((y,h))_{(y,h)\in B}

with
B\subset\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{c}
. We reindex this subsubsequence just by
n
for ease of notation and have

\displaystyle\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{n}_{0}})}\left|\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[f\left((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B_{n}}\right)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[f((Z_{b}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B_{n}})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt\leq\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{n}_{0}})}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[\left|f\left((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B_{n}}\right)-f\left((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{n}_{0}})}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[\left|f\left((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B}\right)-f\left((Z_{b}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{n}_{0}})}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[\left|f\left((Z_{b}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B}\right)-f\left((Z_{b}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B_{n}}\right)\right|\right].

The second summand tends to zero due to our previous work on uniform weak convergence of a finite-dimensional marginal, see (
F.10
). For the first term we use the fact that
f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{n}_{0}})
is Lipschitz continuous with constant one and that all norms on
\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{n}_{0}}
are equivalent. Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{n}_{0}})}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[\left|f\left((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B_{n}}\right)-f\left((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq C^{\prime}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[\left\|\left((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B_{n}}\right)-\left((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B}\right)\right\|_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq C^{\prime}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[\left\|\left((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B_{n}}\right)-\left((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]}

for some constant
C^{\prime}>0
. Denote
B_{n}=\left\{(y_{n}^{1},h_{n}^{1}),\dots,(y_{n}^{\tilde{n}_{0}},h_{n}^{\tilde{n}_{0}})\right\}
. Using Itô’s isometry, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[\left\|\left((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B_{n}}\right)-\left((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\tilde{n}_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T_{n}}}\int_{0}^{T_{n}}\left(K_{y_{n}^{k},h_{n}^{k}}(X_{s})-K_{y_{0}^{k},h_{0}^{k}}(X_{s})\right)dW_{s}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\tilde{n}_{0}}\left\|K_{y_{n}^{k},h_{n}^{k}}-K_{y_{0}^{k},h_{0}^{k}}\right\|_{[-A,A]}^{2}.

This last term tends to zero for
n\to\infty
as the location parameters of the kernel function converge to each other. The third term can be treated in the same way, where we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[\left\|\left((Z_{b}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B_{n}}\right)-\left((Z_{b}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\tilde{n}_{0}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(K_{y_{n}^{k},h_{n}^{k}}(z)-K_{y_{0}^{k},h_{0}^{k}}(z)\right)\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}dW_{z}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq L^{*}\sum_{k=1}^{\tilde{n}_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(K_{y_{n}^{k},h_{n}^{k}}(z)-K_{y_{0}^{k},h_{0}^{k}}(z)\right)^{2}dz.

Here,
L^{*}
is the uniform upper bound on the invariant density
q_{b}
from Lemma
D.1
and again this expression goes to zero due to convergence of the location parameters. Altogether we have convergence to zero of the term

\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{n}_{0}})}\left|\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[f\left((Z_{T_{n}}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B_{n}}\right)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[f((Z_{b}(y,h))_{(y,h)\in B})\right]\right|,

contradicting our choice of
T_{n}
and
B_{n}
that makes this term be bounded from below by
\epsilon
, see (
F.11
). Hence, condition (a) of Proposition
7.3
is fullfilled.

We turn to condition (b). We start proving it for
Z_{b}
and the metric
\overline{\rho}_{b}
. Here we have (with suppressed dependence of
\overline{\rho}_{b}
on its parameters for shorter notation)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\rho}_{b}((y,h),(y^{\prime},h^{\prime}))\leq\delta}\left|Z_{b}(y,h)-Z_{b}(y^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\rho}_{b}((y,h),(y^{\prime},h^{\prime}))\leq\delta}\frac{\left|Z_{b}(y,h)-Z_{b}(y^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|}{\overline{\rho}_{b}\log(e/\overline{\rho}_{b})}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\left.\cdot\sup_{\overline{\rho}_{b}((y,h),(y^{\prime},h^{\prime}))\leq\delta}\overline{\rho}_{b}\log(e/\overline{\rho}_{b})>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(Q\cdot\sup_{\overline{\rho}_{b}((y,h),(y^{\prime},h^{\prime}))\leq\delta}\overline{\rho}_{b}\log(e/\overline{\rho}_{b})>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\rho}_{b}((y,h),(y^{\prime},h^{\prime}))\leq\delta}\frac{\left|Z_{b}(y,h)-Z_{b}(y^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|}{\overline{\rho}_{b}\log(e/\overline{\rho}_{b})}>Q\right)

for any
Q>0
. The first term tends to zero uniformly in
b
as
\delta\searrow 0
because
x\log(e/x)
does for
x\searrow 0
monotonously on
(0,1)
. For a proper choice of the constant
Q
, we have seen in the proof of Theorem
E.4
in equation (
E.8
) that the second term is bounded by
\frac{L^{\prime\prime}\delta}{2}
for some constant
L^{\prime\prime}
not depending on
\delta,b
. Note that Theorem
E.4
is applicable in this setting and in consequence,

\lim_{\delta\searrow}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\rho}_{b}((y,h),(y^{\prime},h^{\prime}))\leq\delta}\left|Z_{b}(y,h)-Z_{b}(y^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|>\epsilon\right)=0.

To show condition (b) for the process
Z_{T}
, we first switch to the semimetric
\overline{\rho}_{T}
instead of
\overline{\rho}_{b}
in the following way (where again the dependence of
\overline{\rho}_{b},\overline{\rho}_{T}
on its parameters is suppressed in the notation):

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\rho}_{b}\leq\delta}\left|Z_{T}(y,h)-Z_{T}(y^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\rho}_{b}^{2}\leq\delta^{2}}\left|Z_{T}(y,h)-Z_{T}(y^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|>\epsilon,\|\overline{\rho}_{T}^{2}-\overline{\rho}_{b}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{T}_{c}\times\mathcal{T}_{c}}\leq\delta^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98866pt+\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\rho}_{b}^{2}\leq\delta^{2}}\left|Z_{T}(y,h)-Z_{T}(y^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|>\epsilon,\|\overline{\rho}_{T}^{2}-\overline{\rho}_{b}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{T}_{c}\times\mathcal{T}_{c}}>\delta^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\rho}_{T}\leq\sqrt{2}\delta}\left|Z_{T}(y,h)-Z_{T}(y^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|>\epsilon\right)+\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\|\overline{\rho}_{T}^{2}-\overline{\rho}_{b}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{T}_{c}\times\mathcal{T}_{c}}>\delta^{2}\right).

By Lemma
F.8
,

\lim_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\|\overline{\rho}_{T}^{2}-\overline{\rho}_{b}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{T}_{c}\times\mathcal{T}_{c}}>\delta^{2}\right)=0.

The first summand can be treated in the same way as above where we established the uniform asymptotic stochastic equicontinuity for
Z_{b}
and is seen to be bounded from above by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(Q\cdot\sup_{\overline{\rho}_{T}((y,h),(y^{\prime},h^{\prime}))\leq\sqrt{2}\delta}\overline{\rho}_{T}\log(e/\overline{\rho}_{T})>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\rho}_{T}((y,h),(y^{\prime},h^{\prime}))\leq\sqrt{2}\delta}\frac{\left|Z_{T}(y,h)-Z_{T}(y^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|}{\overline{\rho}_{T}\log(e/\overline{\rho}_{T})}>Q\right).

The first term again vanishes uniformly in
b
as
\delta\searrow 0
because
x\log(e/x)\to 0
for
x\searrow 0
monotonously on
(0,1)
. For a proper choice of the constant
Q
, we have seen in the proof of Theorem
E.4
in equation (
E.8
) that the second summand is bounded by
\frac{L^{\prime\prime}\delta}{2}+\mathbb{P}_{b}(\mathcal{C}_{T,b}^{c})
for some constant
L^{\prime\prime}
not depending on
\delta,T,b
and the set
\mathcal{C}_{T,b}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{T,b}:=\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}T}L_{T}^{\cdot}(X)-q_{b}\right\|_{[-A,A]}\leq\frac{1}{2}L_{*}\right\}.

(F.12)

Applicability of Theorem
E.4
for
Z_{T}
with this choice of
\mathcal{C}_{T}
was checked in the proof of Theorem
3.1
. Since

\lim_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\mathcal{C}_{T,b}^{c}\right)=0

by (
D.1
), uniform stochastic equicontinuity of
Z_{T}
follows, i.e.

\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\rho}_{b}\leq\delta}\left|Z_{T}(y,h)-Z_{T}(y^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|>\epsilon\right)=0

and condition (b) of Proposition
7.3
is fullfilled.

For condition (c) we first note that
N(u,\mathcal{T}_{c},\overline{\rho}_{b})\leq D(u,\mathcal{T}_{c},\overline{\rho}_{b})
. Then a uniform bound on the capacity numbers follows similarly to that given in the verification of (d) in the proof of Theorem
3.1
for
\mathcal{T}\supset\mathcal{T}_{c}
.

Consequently, by Proposition
7.3
,

\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(l_{\infty}(\mathcal{T}_{c}))}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f((Z_{T}(y,h)_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{c}})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[f((Z_{b}(y,h)_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{c}})\right]\right|

tends to zero for
T\to\infty
and step (i) is complete.

Step (ii).
We set
c=2AL_{*}(L^{*}\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2})^{-1}\delta^{2}
, which is smaller than
A
for every
\delta<c^{\prime}:=\|K\|_{L^{2}}\sqrt{L^{*}/(2L_{*})}
. Using the upper and lower bound of the invariant density from Lemma
D.1
,

\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}:=\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)^{2}q_{b}(z)dz}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{1}_{[-A,A]}(z)q_{b}(z)dz}\leq\frac{L^{*}h\int_{\mathbb{R}}K(z)^{2}dz}{2AL_{*}}.

Consequently,
\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)\geq\delta
implies
h\geq c
and

\displaystyle\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}:\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)>\delta\})}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f((Z_{T}(y,h))_{\{(y,h):\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)>\delta\}})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 184.9429pt\left.\left.-f((Z_{b}(y,h))_{\{(y,h):\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)>\delta\}})\right]\right|

converges to zero for
T\to\infty
by step (i). By the reverse triangle inequality, for
a,b\in l_{\infty}(\mathcal{S})
and
\delta>0
, the map
l_{\infty}(\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with

(x(s))_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\ \mapsto\ \sup_{s\in\mathcal{S}:a(s)>\delta}\left|\frac{x(s)}{a(s)}-b(s)\right|

is Lipschitz continuous with respect to the supremum metric
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{S}}
on
l_{\infty}(\mathcal{S})
. Hence, by Lemma
F.3
it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}:\\
\delta<\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\left|Z_{T}(y,h)\right|}{\sigma_{b}(y,h)}-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\sigma_{b}(y,h)^{2}}{\sigma_{b,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\right)

(F.13)

converges uniformly to
S_{b}(\delta,1)
. To establish the weak convergence of
T_{T}(\delta,1)
to
S_{b}(\delta,1)
for any
0<\delta<c^{\prime}
from this finding, we apply Lemma
F.7
to
Z_{T}
and
Z_{b}
with the specifications

\alpha_{T}(y,h)=\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)\hskip 10.00002pt\textrm{ and }\hskip 10.00002pt\alpha(y,h)=\sigma_{b}(y,h),

and

\beta_{T}(y,h)=\Upsilon\left(\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}/\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}\right)\ \ \textrm{ and }\ \ \beta(y,h)=\Upsilon\left(\sigma_{b}(y,h)^{2}/\sigma_{b,\max}^{2}\right).

Its first condition (a) is true by the convergence of the term in (
F.13
) to
S_{b}(\delta,1)
. Uniform tightness of
S_{b}(\delta,1)
follows from Theorem
E.6
by noting that within its proof the capacity numbers can be bounded similarly as in (
E.14
) with a bound that is independent of
b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)
. Moreover, tightness of
\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}:\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)>\delta}\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})
is clear, as
\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})
is bounded by
\Upsilon(\delta^{2})
for
\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)>\delta
, because
\Upsilon(\cdot)
is decreasing on
[0,1]
. In particular, assumption (b) of Lemma
F.7
holds and it remains to establish assumption (c) and (d) of it, i.e. to prove that for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)>\delta}\left|\frac{\sigma_{b}(y,h)}{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)}-1\right|>\epsilon\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle T\to\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}\ 0

(F.14)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)\geq\delta}\left|\Upsilon\left(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\right)-\Upsilon\left(\frac{\sigma_{b}(y,h)^{2}}{\sigma_{b,\max}^{2}}\right)\right|>\epsilon\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle T\to\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}\ 0.

(F.15)

On the set
\mathcal{C}_{T,b}
defined in (
F.12
) we have
\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}T}L_{T}^{\cdot}\geq\frac{1}{2}q_{b}
and consequently

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}=\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)^{2}\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}T}L_{T}^{z}(X)dz}\geq 2^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}},

or equivalently
\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)\geq 2^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{b}(y,h)
. From
\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)>\delta
, it follows that
\sigma_{b}(y,h)^{2}>\sigma_{b,\max}^{2}\delta^{2}\geq 2AL_{*}\delta^{2}
. Thus, we get on
\mathcal{C}_{T,b}
, applying a first order Taylor expansion of
\sqrt{x}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)>\delta}\left|\frac{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{\rho_{b_{0}}}\|_{L^{2}}}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}}-1\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt=\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)>\delta}\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)}\left|\sigma_{b}(y,h)-\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\leq\frac{1}{2AL_{*}\delta^{2}}\cdot\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)>\delta}\left|\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}-\sigma_{b}(y,h)^{2}\right|,

where we used
1\geq\int_{-A}^{A}q_{b}(z)dz\geq 2AL_{*}
. This term converges to zero uniformly in probability by Proposition
D.4
. Together with the fact that
\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}(\mathcal{C}_{T,b}^{c})\rightarrow 0
by (
D.1
), we have for any
\epsilon>0
that

\displaystyle\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)>\delta}\left|\frac{\sigma_{b}(y,h)}{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)}-1\right|>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\left\{\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)>\delta}\left|\frac{\sigma_{b}(y,h)}{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)}-1\right|>\epsilon\right\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{T,b}\right)+\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\mathcal{C}_{T,b}^{c}\right)

converges to zero for
T\to\infty
and (
F.14
) holds.

To verify (
F.15
), we use that for
\epsilon>0
and
\delta^{\prime}>\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)\geq\delta}\left|\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}\right)-\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}\right)\right|>\epsilon\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\delta<\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)\leq\delta^{\prime}}\left|\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}\right)-\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}\right)\right|>\epsilon\right)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)\geq\delta^{\prime}}\left|\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}\right)-\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}\right)\right|>\epsilon\right).\end{split}

(F.16)

Next, we apply a Taylor expansion for
\Upsilon(r)=\sqrt{2\log(1/r)}
with derivative
\Upsilon^{\prime}(r)=-(r\Upsilon(r))^{-1}
.
The function
r\Upsilon(r)
has its zeros in the endpoints of the interval
[0,1]
, but the absolute value of the derivative is bounded on the compact interval
[\delta,\delta^{\prime}]
by some constant
c(\delta,\delta^{\prime})
. Hence,

\displaystyle\sup_{\delta<\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)\leq\delta^{\prime}}\left|\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}\right)-\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leq c(\delta,\delta^{\prime})\cdot\sup_{\delta<\sigma_{b}(y,h)\leq\delta^{\prime}}\left|\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}-\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}\right|,

which converges to zero in probability, uniformly in
\Sigma
, by Lemma
F.8
. Consequently, the first summand of the right-hand side of (
F.16
) converges to zero, uniformly in
b
, for
T\to\infty
and we now treat the second one. As
\Upsilon(\cdot)
is monotonously decreasing on
[0,1]
, for any
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)\geq\delta^{\prime}}\left|\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}\right)-\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}\right)\right|>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)\geq\delta^{\prime}}\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}\right)+\Upsilon\left((\delta^{\prime})^{2}\right)>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)\geq\delta^{\prime}}\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}+(\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}-\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left.{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\frac{1}{2}}+\Upsilon\left((\delta^{\prime})^{2}\right)>\epsilon,\|\overline{\sigma}_{T}^{2}-\overline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{T}}<\epsilon^{\prime}\right)+\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\|\overline{\sigma}_{T}^{2}-\overline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{T}}\geq\epsilon^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\Upsilon\left((\delta^{\prime})^{2}-\epsilon^{\prime}\right)+\Upsilon\left((\delta^{\prime})^{2}\right)>\epsilon\right)+\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\|\overline{\sigma}_{T}^{2}-\overline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{T}}\geq\epsilon^{\prime}\right).

The latter summand vanishes uniformly over
\Sigma
for
T\to\infty
by Lemma
F.8
, the first one is neither depending on
T
nor probabilistic. Letting
\epsilon^{\prime}\searrow 0
and
\delta^{\prime}\nearrow 1
, it converges to zero because
\Upsilon(r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r\to 1}}{{\longrightarrow}}0
, and we have shown (
F.15
). In summary, by Lemma
F.7
we have proven so far that

\sup_{b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)}d_{BL}^{b}\left(T_{T}(\delta,1),S_{b}(\delta,1)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle T\to\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

Step (iii).
To improve the result from (ii) to uniform weak convergence of
T_{T}(0,1)
to
S_{b}(0,1)
, we split

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)}d_{BL}^{b}\left(T_{T}(0,1),S_{b}(0,1)\right)\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt=\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R})}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\left[f(T_{T}(0,1))-f(S_{b}(0,1))\right]\right|\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt\leq\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R})}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\left[\left|f(T_{T}(0,1))-f(T_{T}(\delta,1))\right|\right]\\
&\hskip 71.13188pt+\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R})}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\left[f(T_{T}(\delta,1))-f(S_{b}(\delta,1))\right]\right|\\
&\hskip 71.13188pt+\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R})}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\left[\left|f(S_{b}(\delta,1))-f(S_{b}(0,1))\right|\right].\end{split}

(F.17)

The second summand equals
\sup_{b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)}d_{BL}^{b}\left(T_{T}(\delta,1),S_{b}(\delta,1)\right)
and we have seen in step (ii) that it vanishes for
T\to\infty
. The first summand is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R})}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\left[\left|f(T_{T}(0,1))-f(T_{T}(\delta,1))\right|\mathbbm{1}_{\{T_{T}(0,1)-T_{T}(\delta,1)>\epsilon\}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}(\mathbb{R})}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\left[\left|f(T_{T}(0,1))-f(T_{T}(\delta,1))\right|\mathbbm{1}_{\{T_{T}(0,1)-T_{T}(\delta,1)\leq\epsilon\}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\epsilon+2\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(T_{T}(0,1)-T_{T}(\delta,1)>\epsilon\right).

Writing
T_{T}(\delta,\delta^{\prime})=\sup_{\delta<\overline{\sigma}(y,h)\leq\delta^{\prime}}A_{T}(y,h)
, we can proceed with

\displaystyle\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(T_{T}(0,1)-T_{T}(\delta,1)>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\max\left\{\sup_{0<\overline{\sigma}(y,h)\leq\delta}A_{T}(y,h),\sup_{\delta<\overline{\sigma}(y,h)\leq 1}A_{T}(y,h)\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 184.9429pt\left.-\sup_{\delta<\overline{\sigma}(y,h)\leq 1}A_{T}(y,h)>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\max\left\{\sup_{0<\overline{\sigma}(y,h)\leq\delta}A_{T}(y,h)-\sup_{\delta<\overline{\sigma}(y,h)\leq 1}A_{T}(y,h),0\right\}>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\max\{\dots\}>\epsilon,\sup_{\delta<\overline{\sigma}(y,h)\leq 1}A_{T}(y,h)\geq-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\max\{\dots\}>\epsilon,\sup_{\delta<\overline{\sigma}(y,h)\leq 1}A_{T}(y,h)<-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{0<\overline{\sigma}(y,h)\leq\delta}A_{T}(y,h)>\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\delta<\overline{\sigma}(y,h)\leq 1}A_{T}(y,h)<-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right).

We start by treating the first summand and show that for all
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(T_{T}(0,\delta)\geq\epsilon\right)=0.

(F.18)

This is based on Theorem
E.4
. First of all,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(T_{T}(0,\delta)\geq\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(T_{T}(0,\delta)\geq\epsilon,\|\overline{\sigma}_{T}^{2}-\overline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{T}}\leq\delta\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(T_{T}(0,\delta)\geq\epsilon,\|\overline{\sigma}_{T}^{2}-\overline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{T}}>\delta\right),

where the second summand is bounded by
\mathbb{P}_{b}(\|\overline{\sigma}_{T}^{2}-\overline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{T}}>\delta)
and vanishes uniformly in
b
for
T\to\infty
as shown in Lemma
F.8
. For the other one, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(T_{T}(0,\delta)\geq\epsilon,\|\overline{\sigma}_{T}^{2}-\overline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{T}}\leq\delta^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}\leq\delta^{2}}A_{T}(y,h)>\epsilon,\|\overline{\sigma}_{T}^{2}-\overline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{T}}\leq\delta^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}\leq 2\delta^{2}}\frac{A_{T}(y,h)}{D(\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h))}\cdot D(\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h))>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)\leq\sqrt{2}\delta}\frac{A_{T}(y,h)}{D(\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h))}\cdot\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)\leq\sqrt{2}\delta}D(\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h))>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(L\cdot\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)\leq\sqrt{2}\delta}D(\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h))>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)\leq\sqrt{2}\delta}\frac{A_{T}(y,h)}{D(\overline{\sigma}_{T}(y,h))}>L\right)

for any constant
L>0
. Now, the first summand here tends to zero for
\delta\searrow 0
as
D(r)\searrow 0
monotonously for
r\searrow 0
. The random variable in the second probability is uniformly asymptotically tight by Theorem
E.4
and thus vanishes in the limit
\lim_{L\to\infty}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b}
. Note that the applicability of Theorem
E.4
was checked in the proof of Theorem
3.1
. For the uniformity mentioned before, note that all bounds used in the proof of Theorem
3.1
, in particular the last one of (
E.14
), are independent of
b
.

It remains to deal for all
\epsilon>0
with the term

\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(T_{T}(\delta,1)<-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right).

Define the Lipschitz function

g_{\epsilon}(y):=\begin{cases}1&\textrm{ if }x\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2},\\
1-4\frac{y+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}{\epsilon}&\textrm{ if }-\frac{\epsilon}{2}<y<-\frac{\epsilon}{4},\\
0&\textrm{ if }x\geq-\frac{\epsilon}{4}.\end{cases}

Then we have

\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{x<-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right\}}\leq g_{\epsilon}(x)\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{x<-\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right\}}

and consequently,

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(T_{T}(\delta,1)<-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[g_{\epsilon}(T_{T}(\delta,1))\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt=\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[g_{\epsilon}(S_{b}(\delta,1))\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[g_{\epsilon}(T_{T}(\delta,1))\right]-\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[g_{\epsilon}(S_{b}(\delta,1))\right].

By the already established uniform weak convergence of
T_{T}(\delta,1)
to
S_{b}(\delta,1)
and the fact that
g_{\epsilon}
is a bounded Lipschitz function, we get for the difference

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[g_{\epsilon}(T_{T}(\delta,1))\right]-\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[g_{\epsilon}(S_{b}(\delta,1))\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\left|\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[g_{\epsilon}(T_{T}(\delta,1))\right]-\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[g_{\epsilon}(S_{b}(\delta,1))\right]\right|=0

and we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(T_{T}(\delta,1)<-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[g_{\epsilon}(S_{b}(\delta,1))\right]

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(S_{b}(\delta,1)<-\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right).

Set
\tilde{\delta}:=4\delta^{2}(2AL^{*})(L_{*}\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2})^{-1}

and define for
-\frac{A}{\tilde{\delta}}\leq k\leq\frac{A}{\tilde{\delta}}
,

Z_{k}(b):=\|K_{k\tilde{\delta},\frac{\tilde{\delta}}{2}}\sqrt{\rho_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{k\tilde{\delta},\frac{\tilde{\delta}}{2}}(z)\sqrt{\rho_{b}(z)}dW_{z}.

Those random variables are independent, as the support of the functions
K_{k\tilde{\delta},\tilde{\delta}/2}
and
K_{k^{\prime}\tilde{\delta},\tilde{\delta}/2}
is disjoint for
k\neq k^{\prime}
. Moreover, they are standard Gaussians, in particular their distribution does not depend on
b
, and hence their maximum is
(2\log(2A\tilde{\delta}^{-1}))^{\frac{1}{2}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
for
\delta\searrow 0
(see
[
35
]
, Theorem
1.5.3
). Using the uniform bound on the invariant density from Lemma
D.1
,

\frac{\|K_{k\tilde{\delta},\frac{\tilde{\delta}}{2}}\sqrt{\rho_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{\|\mathbbm{1}_{[-A,A]}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\geq\frac{L_{*}\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{2AL^{*}}\frac{\tilde{\delta}}{2}=2\delta^{2}.

As
\Upsilon(\cdot)
is monotonously decreasing on
[0,1]
,

\displaystyle S_{b}(\delta,1)

\displaystyle\geq\max_{-A\tilde{\delta}^{-1}\leq k\leq A\tilde{\delta}^{-1}}\left(Z_{k}(b)-\Upsilon\left(\frac{\|K_{k\tilde{\delta},\frac{\tilde{\delta}}{2}}\sqrt{\rho_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{\|\mathbbm{1}_{[-A,A]}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\max_{-A\tilde{\delta}^{-1}\leq k\leq A\tilde{\delta}^{-1}}Z_{k}(b)-\Upsilon(2\delta^{2})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2\log(2A\tilde{\delta}^{-1})}-\sqrt{2\log((2\delta^{2})^{-1})}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=o_{\delta}(1)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\hskip 10.00002pt\textrm{ for }\delta\searrow 0,

Thus, we finally get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(S_{b}(\delta,1)<-\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right)\\
&\hskip 8.5359pt\leq\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\max_{-A\tilde{\delta}^{-1}\leq k\leq A\tilde{\delta}^{-1}}\left(Z_{k}(b)-\Upsilon\left(\frac{\|K_{k\tilde{\delta},\frac{\tilde{\delta}}{2}}\sqrt{\rho_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{\|\mathbbm{1}_{[-A,A]}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\right)\right)<-\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right)\\
&\hskip 8.5359pt\leq\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{-A\tilde{\delta}^{-1}\leq k\leq A\tilde{\delta}^{-1}}Z_{k}(b)-\Upsilon(2\delta^{2})<-\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right)=0\end{split}

(F.19)

and the convergence of the first summand on the right-hand side in (
F.17
) to zero for
\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}
is shown. It remains to study the third summand in (
F.17
), i.e.

\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{BL}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\left[\left|f(S_{b}(\delta,1))-f(S_{b}(0,1))\right|\right].

This is done in the same way as for the first summand and is only easier, as the
\limsup_{T\to\infty}
is omitted and the argument reduces to additionally showing that for any
\epsilon>0
,

\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in\Sigma}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(S_{b}(0,\delta)\geq\epsilon\right)=0.

This follows along the lines of the previous part as Theorem
E.4
is also applicable to
S_{b}
.
∎

Remark F.9
.

Let
c>0
and
\mathcal{T}_{c}
be the set given in (
F.8
). For any subset
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{T}_{c}
define

\displaystyle T_{T}(\mathcal{T}^{\prime}):=\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\left|Z_{T}(y,h)\right|}{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)}-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\right)

as well as

\displaystyle S_{b}(\mathcal{T}^{\prime}):=\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\left|Z_{b}(y,h)\right|}{\sigma_{b}(y,h)}-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\sigma_{b}(y,h)^{2}}{\sigma_{b,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\right).

Then our proof of Theorem
7.1
even shows that

\sup_{b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)}d_{BL}^{b}\left(T_{T}(\mathcal{T}^{\prime}),S_{b}(\mathcal{T}^{\prime})\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle T\to\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

This can be seen as follows: Step (i) directly transfers and in step (ii) we have to show (
F.14
) and (
F.15
) for the supremum over
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, which is straightforward by the same lines. Step (iii) can be omitted since
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{T}_{c}
.

G
Proofs of Section
4

In Subsection
G.1
the proof of Theorem
4.1
is given. This proof is sketched in Section
7
and strongly relies on the uniform weak convergence result for the test statistic
T_{T}^{b}
given in Theorem
7.1
that was proven in Subsection
F.2
. In the subsequent Subsection
G.2
we present a proof of Remark
4.2
, i.e. we prove that the distribution of the dominating random variable
U_{1}\vee U_{2}+4\sqrt{A\eta/\sigma^{2}}
from Theorem
4.1
has no point mass.

G.1
Proof of Theorem
4.1

Let
b\in H_{0}(b_{0},\eta)
. For ease of notation we abbreviate
H_{0}(b_{0},\eta)
by
H_{0}
in what follows. As already indicated in Section
7
, the key idea of the proof is to split the set
\mathcal{T}
in three parts (two of them depending on
b
). For a rigorous description of those, we choose
\epsilon^{\prime}>\epsilon>0
and define the set

U_{\epsilon,y,h}(b):=\left\{x\in[y-h,y+h]:q_{b}(x)\left(|b(x)-b_{0}(x)|-\eta\right)<-\epsilon\right\}

and the partition

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\left\{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}\mid h<\epsilon^{\prime}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\left\{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}\mid h\geq\epsilon^{\prime}\textrm{ and }\lambda\left(U_{\epsilon,y,h}(b)\right)\geq\epsilon\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{3}

\displaystyle:=\left\{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}\mid h\geq\epsilon^{\prime}\textrm{ and }\lambda\left(U_{\epsilon,y,h}(b)\right)<\epsilon\right\}.

Note the dependences
\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{1}(\epsilon^{\prime})
,
\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{2}(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},b)
and
\mathcal{T}_{3}=\mathcal{T}_{3}(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},b)
, which most times will be suppressed in the notation to allow for shorter displays. With these definitions and the union bound,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}_{b}(T_{T}^{\eta}(X)\geq r)\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt=\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}}\left(|\Psi_{T,y,h}^{b_{0}}(X)|-\Lambda_{T,y,h}^{\eta}(X)-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\right)\geq r\right)\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\left(|\Psi_{T,y,h}^{b_{0}}(X)|-\Lambda_{T,y,h}^{\eta}(X)-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\right)\geq r\right).\end{split}

(G.1)

Of course, the inequality remains valid if we put

\limsup_{\epsilon^{\prime}\searrow 0}\limsup_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}(b_{0},\eta)}

before both sides. The left-hand side of (
G.1
) does not depend on
\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}
and
\limsup_{T}\sup_{b}\mathbb{P}_{b}(T_{T}^{\eta}(X)\geq r)
equals the left-hand side of the statement of the theorem. For the rest of the proof, we define the set

\mathcal{C}_{T,b}:=\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}T}L_{T}^{\cdot}(X)-q_{b}\right\|_{[-A,A]}\leq\frac{1}{2}L_{*}\right\}.

Note that on
\mathcal{C}_{T,b}
we have

\frac{1}{2}q_{b}(z)\leq\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}T}L_{T}^{z}(X)\leq\frac{3}{2}q_{b}(z),\hskip 10.00002ptz\in[-A,A],

for each drift
b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)
and
\lim_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)}\mathbb{P}_{b}(\mathcal{C}_{T,b}^{c})=0
holds by (
D.1
).
Note that on
\mathcal{C}_{T,b}
, the term
|\Psi_{T,y,h}^{b_{0}}(X)|-\Lambda_{T,y,h}^{\eta}(X)
is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})dW_{s}}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}}\right|\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})(b(X_{s})-b_{0}(X_{s}))ds\right|-\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{T}}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})ds}{\sigma\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}}.\end{split}

(G.2)

Using the triangle inequality, the numerator of the second summand is smaller than

\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})\left(|b(X_{s})-b_{0}(X_{s})|-\eta\right)ds

and this term is smaller than or equal to zero for each
b\in H_{0}(b_{0},\eta)
. Consequently, the whole second summand in (
G.2
) is non-positive. Here, we used the assumption
K\geq 0
. With this preliminary observation, we give the heuristic about the decomposition of
\mathcal{T}
into
\mathcal{T}_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
, and the route of the rest of the proof:

•

The probability in (
G.1
) with
\mathcal{T}_{1}
is neglectable in the limit
\lim_{\epsilon^{\prime}\searrow 0}
which can be seen using the same techniques from empirical process theory as for the proof of Theorem
3.1
.

•

For
(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{2}
,
|b-b_{0}|-\eta
is sufficiently bounded away from zero on a subinterval of
[y-h,y+h]
such that the second summand in (
G.2
) tends to
-\infty
, whereas the first one remains stochastically bounded, uniformly in
b
. Hence, the corresponding probability in (
G.1
) tends to zero.

•

The probability with
\mathcal{T}_{3}
in (
G.1
) does not vanish in the limit and is estimated against
\mathbb{P}(U_{1}\vee U_{2}+4\sqrt{A\eta/\sigma^{2}}\geq r)
. In particular, it is the origin of the correction term
4\sqrt{A\eta/\sigma^{2}}
.

From (
F.19
) in step (iii) of the proof of Theorem
7.1
it can be seen that both random variables
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
are
\geq 0
almost surely and consequently
U_{1}\vee U_{2}+4\sqrt{A\eta/\sigma^{2}}>0
for
\eta>0
. Therefore, it suffices to consider
r>0
in the following, where we will estimate each summand on the right-hand side of (
G.1
) separately.

Step (i).
By neglecting the non-positive second summand in (
G.2
), for
b\in H_{0}
we may estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{1}}\left(|\Psi_{T,y,h}^{b_{0}}(X)|-\Lambda_{T,y,h}^{\eta}(X)-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\right)\geq r\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{1}}\left(\left|\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})dW_{s}}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}}\right|-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\right)\geq r,\mathcal{C}_{T,b}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\mathcal{C}_{T,b}^{c}\right).

The second summand vanishes in the limit
T\to\infty
uniformly over the drift
b
. For the first one, note that
h<\epsilon^{\prime}
implies

\frac{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{\|\mathbbm{1}_{[-A,A]}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}=\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)^{2}q_{b}(z)dz}{\int_{-A}^{A}q_{b}(z)dz}\leq\frac{L^{*}h\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{2AL_{*}}<\frac{L^{*}\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\epsilon^{\prime}}{2AL_{*}}=:c\epsilon^{\prime}

by using the uniform upper and lower bound of the invariant density
q_{b}
given in Lemma
D.1
.
Then, with
T_{T}(\delta,\delta^{\prime})
given in (
F.9
) within the proof of Theorem
7.1
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon^{\prime}\searrow 0}\limsup_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{1}}\left(\Psi_{T,y,h}^{b_{0}}(X)-\Lambda_{T,y,h}^{\eta}(X)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt\left.\left.-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\right)\geq r,\mathcal{C}_{T,b}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\limsup_{\epsilon^{\prime}\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}\left(T_{T}\left(0,\sqrt{c\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)\geq r\right)=0,

where we used that
\mathcal{T}_{1}
does not depend on
\epsilon
and the last equality was proven in step (iii) of the proof of Theorem
7.1
, see (
F.18
).

Step (ii).
With (
G.2
), we bound the supremum taken over
\mathcal{T}_{2}
on
\mathcal{C}_{T,b}
by

\displaystyle\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{2}}\left(|\Psi_{T,y,h}^{b_{0}}(X)|-\Lambda_{T,y,h}^{\eta}(X)-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{2}}\left(\left|\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})dW_{s}}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}}\right|-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{2}}\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})\left(|b(X_{s})-b_{0}(X_{s})|-\eta\right)ds}{\sigma\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}}.

The limiting distribution of the first term is given by Theorem
7.1
together with Remark
F.9
, in particular it is asymptotically tight uniformly in
b\in H_{0}
. We are going to show convergence of the second term to
-\infty
in probability uniformly in
b\in H_{0}
on
\mathcal{C}_{T,b}
. To proceed, we first write with the occupation times formula,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})(|b(X_{s})-b_{0}(X_{s})|-\eta)ds}{\sigma\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt=\sqrt{T}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)(|b(z)-b_{0}(z)|-\eta)\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}T}L_{T}^{z}(X)dz}{\sigma\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)^{2}\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}T}L_{T}^{z}(X)dz}}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\sqrt{T}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)(|b(z)-b_{0}(z)|-\eta)\frac{1}{2}q_{b}(z)dz}{\sigma\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)^{2}\cdot\frac{3}{2}q_{b}(z)dz}}\end{split}

(G.3)

In the third step we used that
|b-b_{0}|-\eta
is non-positive and therefore inserted the lower bound of the empirical density in the nominator and the upper bound in the denominator. Keeping in mind that the last expression in (
G.3
) is negative, bounding it from above is the same as finding lower bounds of the absolute value. In particular, we may choose the denominator as large as possible by using the upper bound
L^{*}
of the invariant density from Lemma
D.1
. Thus, the right-hand side of (
G.3
) is bounded from above by

\frac{\sqrt{T}}{\sqrt{6L^{*}h}\sigma\|K\|_{L^{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)\left(|b(z)-b_{0}(z)|-\eta\right)q_{b}(z)dz.

From our condition on the set
\mathcal{T}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)\left(|b(z)-b_{0}(z)|-\eta\right)q_{b}(z)dz

\displaystyle\leq-\epsilon\int_{U_{\epsilon,y,h}}K_{y,h}(z)dz

\displaystyle=-\epsilon h\int_{(U_{\epsilon,y,h}-y)/h}K(z)dz.

Here, for a set
A
we denote
(A-y)/h:=\{(x-y)/h\mid x\in A\}
. We have
\lambda((U_{\epsilon,y,h}-y)/h)\geq\epsilon/h\geq\epsilon/A
. Hence, the last integral is bounded away from zero by a constant
C_{0}(K)>0
. This gives the bound

\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})(|b(X_{s})-b_{0}(X_{s})|-\eta)ds}{\sigma\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}}\leq-\frac{\sqrt{T\epsilon^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{6L^{*}}\sigma\|K\|_{L^{2}}}C_{0}\epsilon

where we used that
h\geq\epsilon^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{T}_{2}
.
This bound does not depend on the location parameters
(y,h)
and we can put the supremum before the expression on the left-hand side without changing the right-hand side.
In particular, we have the desired uniform convergence in probability

\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{2}}\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})\left(|b(X_{s})-b_{0}(X_{s})|-\eta\right)ds}{\sigma\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{b},\textrm{unif}}\ -\infty

for
T\to\infty
on
\mathcal{C}_{T,b}
. Then we can finalize step (ii) by bounding

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{2}}\left(|\Psi_{T,y,h}^{b_{0}}(X)|-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\bigg)-\Psi_{T}^{\eta}(y,h)\right)\geq r\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\left\{\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{2}}\left(|\Psi_{T,y,h}^{b_{0}}(X)|-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\bigg)-\Psi_{T}^{\eta}(y,h)\right)\geq r\right\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{T,b}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\mathcal{C}_{T,b}^{c}\right)

and applying
\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}
on both sides, which allows to conclude convergence to zero of the right-hand side, and consequently, of the left-hand side, too.

Step (iii).
In this case, we consider the test statistic with the supremum taken over
\mathcal{T}_{3}
without the non-positive second summand in (
G.2
), i.e. we use

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}}\left(|\Psi_{T,y,h}^{b_{0}}(X)|-\Lambda_{T,y,h}^{\eta}(X)-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\right)\geq r\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91684pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}}\left(\frac{\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})dW_{s}\right|}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}}-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\right)\geq r,\mathcal{C}_{T,b}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\mathcal{C}_{T,b}^{c}\right).

The second summand vanishes in the limit
T\to\infty
uniformly in
b
. In the rest of the proof, we will find an upper bound for the first summand in the right-hand side of this estimate in the limit
\limsup_{T}\sup_{b}
. As a starting point, we make use of the weak limit result of Theorem
7.1
in combination with Remark
F.9
and get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{b}\hskip-2.84544pt\left(\hskip-1.42271pt\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}}\hskip-1.42271pt\left(\frac{\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})dW_{s}\right|}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}}\hskip-1.42271pt-\hskip-1.42271pt\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{T}(y,h)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{T,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\hskip-2.84544pt\right)\hskip-1.42271pt\geq\hskip-1.42271ptr,\mathcal{C}_{T,b}\right)\\
&\hskip 2.84544pt\leq\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}}\left(\frac{\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}dW_{z}\right|}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}-\Upsilon\bigg(\frac{\sigma_{b}(y,h)^{2}}{\sigma_{b,\max}^{2}}\bigg)\hskip-1.42271pt\right)\hskip-1.42271pt\geq r-\delta\right)\hskip-1.42271pt,\end{split}

(G.4)

where
\delta>0
is arbitrary. In what follows, we determine
\limsup_{\epsilon\searrow 0}
of the right-hand side. Remember that
\mathcal{T}_{3}=\mathcal{T}_{3}(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},b)
, where this dependence is usually suppressed for smaller displays. The above inequality in (
G.4
) can be seen as follows: Abbreviate both suprema by
A_{T}
and
A
, respectively, and set for
\delta>0
the function
g_{\delta}
as

\displaystyle g_{\delta}(x):=\begin{cases}0,&\textrm{ if }x\leq r-\delta,\\
\frac{x-r+\delta}{\delta},&\textrm{ if }r-\delta<x<r,\\
1,&\textrm{ if }x\geq r.\end{cases}

Then we have,

\displaystyle\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(A_{T}\geq r,\mathcal{C}_{T,b}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt=\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{A_{T}\geq r\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{C}_{T,b}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leq\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[g_{\delta}(A_{T})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{C}_{T,b}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leq\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[g_{\delta}(A)\right]+\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[g_{\delta}(A_{T})-g_{\delta}(A)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[g_{\delta}(A_{T})(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{C}_{T,b}}-1)\right].

The first term is bounded from above by
\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{b}(A\geq r-\delta)
and the second one vanishes by Theorem
7.1
and Remark
F.9
as
g_{\delta}
is a bounded Lipschitz function. As the maximum value of
g_{\delta}
is one, the third summand can be bounded by

\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[\left|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{C}_{T,b}}-1\right|\right]\leq\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\mathcal{C}_{T,b}^{c}\right)=0.

For the following arguments we define

\displaystyle U_{\epsilon,y,h}^{+}(b)

\displaystyle:=\left\{x\in[y-h,y+h]\mid q_{b}(x)\left(b(x)-b_{0}(x)-\eta\right)<-\epsilon\right\},

\displaystyle U_{\epsilon,y,h}^{-}(b)

\displaystyle:=\left\{x\in[y-h,y+h]\mid q_{b}(x)\left(b_{0}(x)-b(x)-\eta\right)<-\epsilon\right\}

and the corresponding sets

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},b)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{T}_{3}(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},b)\cap\left\{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}\mid\lambda\left(U_{\epsilon,y,h}^{+}(b)\right)<\epsilon\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{3}^{-}(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},b)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{T}_{3}(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},b)\cap\left\{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}\mid\lambda\left(U_{\epsilon,y,h}^{-}(b)\right)<\epsilon\right\},

for which we have
\mathcal{T}_{3}(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},b)=\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},b)\cup\mathcal{T}_{3}^{-}(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},b)
.
We now proceed with the right-hand side of (
G.4
). Remember the notation

\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)=\frac{\sigma_{b}(y,h)}{\sigma_{b,\max}}

from (
F.6
) and (
F.7
) and abbreviate

A_{y,h}(b):=\frac{\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}dW_{z}\right|}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}

in what follows. Omitting the dependence of
\mathcal{T}_{3}^{\pm}
on
\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}
and
b
for notational convenience,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}}\left(A_{y,h}(b)-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})\right)\geq r-\delta\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\max\left\{\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left(A_{y,h}(b)-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})\right),\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\left.\left.\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{-}}\left(A_{y,h}(b)-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})\right)\right\}\geq r-\delta\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\hskip-1.42271pt\max\left\{\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left(A_{y,h}(b_{0}+\eta)\hskip-1.42271pt-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})+A_{y,h}(b)-A_{y,h}(b_{0}+\eta)\right),\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\left.\left.\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{-}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left(A_{y,h}(b_{0}-\eta)-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})+A_{y,h}(b)-A_{y,h}(b_{0}-\eta)\right)\hskip-1.42271pt\right\}\hskip-1.42271pt\geq r-\delta\right)\hskip-2.84544pt.

Using the inequality
\max\{a+b,c+d\}\leq\max\{a,c\}+\max\{b,d\}
, we can proceed upper bounding with

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left(A_{y,h}(b_{0}+\eta)-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})\right)\vee\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{-}}\left(A_{y,h}(b_{0}-\eta)-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91684pt\left.+\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left(A_{y,h}(b)-A_{y,h}(b_{0}+\eta)\right)\vee\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{-}}\left(A_{y,h}(b)-A_{y,h}(b_{0}-\eta)\right)\geq r-\delta\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\max\left\{\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left(A_{y,h}(b_{0}+\eta)-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})\right),\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\left.\left.\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{-}}\left(A_{y,h}(b_{0}-\eta)-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})\right)\right\}\geq r-2\delta\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left(A_{y,h}(b)-A_{y,h}(b_{0}+\eta)\right)\vee\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{-}}\left(A_{y,h}(b)-A_{y,h}(b_{0}-\eta)\right)>\delta\right),

where the last line is true for any
\delta>0
because for any real-valued random variables
X,Y
and
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X+Y>a)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(X+Y>a,Y\leq b)+\mathbb{P}(X+Y>a,Y>b)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(X>a-b)+\mathbb{P}(Y>b).

We will consider the asymptotic
\limsup_{\delta\searrow 0}
at the end of the proof. But first, we turn to the second summand. Splitted up by the union bound and written out, it is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left|A_{y,h}(b)-A_{y,h}(b_{0}+\eta)\right|>\delta\right)\hskip-1.42271pt+\hskip-1.42271pt\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{-}}\left|A_{y,h}(b)-A_{y,h}(b_{0}-\eta)\right|>\delta\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)\left(\frac{\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}-\frac{\sqrt{q_{b_{0}+\eta}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b_{0}+\eta}}\|_{L^{2}}}\right)dW_{z}\right|>\delta\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt+\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{-}}\hskip-1.42271pt\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)\left(\frac{\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}\hskip-1.42271pt-\hskip-1.42271pt\frac{\sqrt{q_{b_{0}-\eta}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b_{0}-\eta}}\|_{L^{2}}}\right)dW_{z}\right|>\delta\right)\hskip-1.42271pt,

where the inequality used the reverse triangle inequality. Note, that the preceding inequality still holds true with
\sup_{b\in H_{0}(b_{0},\eta)}
in front of it. Our aim is to show that both probabilities vanish for
\limsup_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in H_{0}(b_{0},\eta)}
. As the procedure is the same for both, we focus on the first one. By Itô’s formula applied to the function
g(x,y)=xy
and the processes
(W_{z})_{a\leq z\leq b}
and
(f(z))_{a\leq z\leq b}
for a continuous function
f
of bounded variation with
\|f\|_{[a,b]}<\infty
, we get

f(b)W_{b}-f(a)W_{a}=\int_{a}^{b}W_{z}df(z)+\int_{a}^{b}f(z)dW_{z}

as the covariation
[f,W]
vanishes. Consequently,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{a}^{b}f(z)dW_{z}\right|\leq\|W\|_{[a,b]}\left(2\|f\|_{\infty}+V_{a}^{b}(f)\right).

(G.5)

Here,
V_{a}^{b}(f)
denotes the variation of
f
on the interval
[a,b]
and we used the classical bound
|\int_{a}^{b}f(x)dg(x)|\leq\|f\|_{[a,b]}V_{a}^{b}(g)
for Stieltjes-integrals (cf.
[
46
]
, Corollary to Theorem
6.29
). We want to apply (
G.5
) to the function

f_{y,h}(z)=K_{y,h}(z)\left(\frac{\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}-\frac{\sqrt{q_{b_{0}+\eta}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b_{0}+\eta}}\|_{L^{2}}}\right).

This function
f_{y,h}
is continuous as
q_{b}
and
K
are continuous. Moreover, the function in brackets is of bounded variation because it is differentiable with bounded derivative (cf.
[
46
]
, Example
6.23
(b)) and it follows that
f_{y,h}
is of bounded variation as a product of two such functions (cf.
[
46
]
, Theorem
6.24
).

To proceed, an intermediate step is necessary. From the explicit representation of the invariant density
q_{b}
we get for
z\in[y-h,y+h]\subset\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}=\frac{\sqrt{\exp\left(\int_{y-h}^{z}2\sigma^{-2}b(u)du\right)}}{\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)^{2}\exp\left(\int_{y-h}^{z}2\sigma^{-2}b(u)du\right)dz}}.

A similar equality holds for
[y-h,y+h]\subset\mathbb{R}_{\leq 0}
, where
y-h
as a boundary value of the integral is replaced by
y+h
. In the third case that
y-h<0
and
y+h>0
, there is no need for splitting the argument of
\exp(\cdot)
in a part depending on
z
and one that does not. In either way, we derived that
q_{b}(z)=c_{y,h}\tilde{q}_{b}(y,h)(z)
on the interval
[y-h,y+h]
for some constant
c_{y,h}
that cancels out in the above fraction and
\tilde{q}_{b}(y,h)
only depends on the values
b(z)
for
z\in[y-h,y+h]
. In particular, this implies that for
b_{1}
and
b_{2}
that are the same on
[y-h,y+h]
, we have
\tilde{q}_{b_{1}}(y,h)=\tilde{q}_{b_{2}}(y,h)
although
q_{b_{1}}\neq q_{b_{2}}
in general. We also have seen

\frac{\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}=\frac{\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b}(y,h)(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b}(y,h)}\|_{L^{2}}}.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle f_{y,h}(z)

\displaystyle=K_{y,h}(z)\left(\frac{\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b}(y,h)(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b}(y,h)}\|_{L^{2}}}-\frac{\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)}\|_{L^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=:K_{y,h}(z)g_{y,h}(z).

Since
\|K\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, we have
\|f_{y,h}\|_{[y-h,y+h]}\leq\|g_{y,h}\|_{[y-h,y+h]}
and for the variation (cf.
[
46
]
, proof of Theorem
6.24
),

\displaystyle V_{y-h}^{y+h}(f_{y,h})

\displaystyle=V_{y-h}^{y+h}(K_{y,h}g_{y,h})

\displaystyle\leq\|K_{y,h}\|_{[y-h,y+h]}V_{y-h}^{y+h}(g_{y,h})+\|g_{y,h}\|_{[y-h,y+h]}V_{y-h}^{y+h}(K_{y,h})

\displaystyle\leq V_{y-h}^{y+h}(g_{y,h})+\|g_{y,h}\|_{[y-h,y+h]}V_{-1}^{1}(K).

So far, (
G.5
) gives the estimate

\displaystyle\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)\left(\frac{\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}-\frac{\sqrt{q_{b_{0}+\eta}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b_{0}+\eta}}\|_{L^{2}}}\right)dW_{z}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left(\|W\|_{[y-h,y+h]}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 65.44142pt\left.\cdot\left(2\|g_{y,h}\|_{[y-h,y+h]}+V_{y-h}^{y+h}(g_{y,h})+\|g_{y,h}\|_{[y-h,y+h]}V_{-1}^{1}(K)\right)\right).

Clearly,
\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\|W\|_{[y-h,y+h]}\leq\|W\|_{[-A,A]}
. The bracket on the right-hand side converges to zero for
\epsilon\searrow 0
if we can establish

(1)

\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},b)}\|g_{y,h}\|_{[y-h,y+h]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\epsilon\searrow 0}}{{\longrightarrow}}0
and

(2)

\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},b)}V_{y-h}^{y+h}(g_{y,h})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\epsilon\searrow 0}}{{\longrightarrow}}0
.

For both convergences the following fact is important. For each
(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}
we have

\int_{y-h}^{y+h}\left|b(y)-b_{0}(y)-\eta\right|dy\leq\frac{2h\epsilon}{L_{*}}+2\eta\epsilon,

because we have
0\geq q_{b}(b-b_{0}-\eta)\geq-\epsilon
on
[y-h,y+h]
except for a set
A\subset[y-h,y+h]
with
\lambda(A)<\epsilon
on which
|b-b_{0}-\eta|\leq 2\eta
as
b\in H_{0}(b_{0},\eta)
. Furthermore, we have bounded
q_{b}\geq L_{*}
by Lemma
D.1
. This implies

\displaystyle\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left\|b-(b_{0}+\eta)\right\|_{L^{1}([y-h,y+h])}\leq\frac{2A\epsilon}{L_{*}}+2\eta\epsilon\ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle\epsilon\searrow 0}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

(G.6)

Now we move on to prove (1) and (2).

(1)

For this claim we will show that both the nominator and denominator of
g_{y,h}
converge to each other uniformly in
(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}
. Then the claim follows by noting that

\inf_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)}\right\|_{L^{2}}>0,

which follows from the fact that
h>\epsilon^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}
and that
\tilde{q}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)
is bounded away from zero for those
h>\epsilon^{\prime}
.

In the following, we suppress the dependence of
\tilde{q}_{b}
on
(y,h)
for better readability. For the denominators we have

\displaystyle\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left|\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b_{0}+\eta}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)^{2}\left(\tilde{q}_{b}(z)-\tilde{q}_{b_{0}+\eta}(z)\right)dz\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left(h\left\|\tilde{q}_{b}-\tilde{q}_{b_{0}+\eta}\right\|_{[y-h,y+h]}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq A\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left\|\tilde{q}_{b}-\tilde{q}_{b_{0}+\eta}\right\|_{[y-h,y+h]}.

To estimate the last term, an explicit representation of
\tilde{q}_{b}
is helpful. We consider, as above, the case where
z\in[y-h,y+h]\subset\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, the others follow along the same lines. In the above case we have
\tilde{q}_{b}(y,h)(z)=\int_{y-h}^{z}2\sigma^{-2}b(u)du
and by Taylor’s formula,

\displaystyle\left\|\exp\left(\int_{y-h}^{\cdot}2\sigma^{-2}b(y)dy\right)-\exp\left(\int_{y-h}^{\cdot}2\sigma^{-2}(b_{0}+\eta)(y)dy\right)\right\|_{[y-h,y+h]}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\exp(\xi)\left\|\int_{y-h}^{\cdot}2\sigma^{-2}\left(b-(b_{0}+\eta)\right)(y)dy\right\|_{[y-h,y+h]}

for some
\xi
between the two integral terms. As the drift is only evaluated for
y
lying in the interval
[-A,A]
, by the at most linear growth condition on
b
we have
|\xi|\leq 2A\sigma^{-2}C(1+A)
.
The remaining supremum can be bounded by (
G.6
) which gives

\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left\|\tilde{q}_{b}-\tilde{q}_{b_{0}+\eta}\right\|_{[y-h,y+h]}\leq 2\sigma^{-2}e^{2A\sigma^{-2}C(1+A)}\left(\frac{2A}{L_{*}}+2\eta\right)\epsilon

and this term goes to zero for
\epsilon\searrow 0
. This finishes the proof of convergence of the denominators in supremum norm and also proves this convergence for the nominators by noting that
\tilde{q}_{b}(y,h)
is bounded from below as was noted at the beginning of this step (1).

(2)

We turn to the second statment and use that
g
is differentiable and we therefore have
V_{a}^{b}(g)=\int_{a}^{b}|g^{\prime}(z)|dz
(cf.
[
46
]
, Theorem
6.35
). Noting that

\frac{d}{dz}\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b}(y,h)(z)}=\sigma^{-2}b(z)\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b}(y,h)(z)}

by
\sigma^{2}q_{b}^{\prime}=2bq_{b}
and
q_{b}(z)=c_{y,h}\tilde{q}_{b}(y,h)(z)
, we thus have

\displaystyle V_{y-h}^{y+h}(g)=\int_{y-h}^{y+h}\left|\frac{b(z)\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b}(y,h)(z)}}{\sigma^{2}\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}-\frac{(b_{0}(z)+\eta)\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)(z)}}{\sigma^{2}\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b_{0}+\eta}}\|_{L^{2}}}\right|dz

By the arguments of part (1) and (
G.6
) we see that

\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\int_{y-h}^{y+h}\left|b(z)\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b}(y,h)(z)}-(b_{0}(z)+\eta)\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)(z)}\right|dz\rightarrow 0

and

\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{\tilde{q}_{b_{0}+\eta}}\|_{L^{2}}}\right)\rightarrow 0

for
\epsilon\searrow 0
which then gives the desired claim (2).

To this end, we can conclude that we find
\tilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon)>0
converging to zero for
\epsilon\searrow 0
such that
\|b-b_{0}-\eta\|_{I}<\epsilon
implies

\displaystyle\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}(\epsilon)}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)\left(\frac{\sqrt{\rho_{b}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{\rho_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}-\frac{\sqrt{\rho_{b_{0}+\eta}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{\rho_{b_{0}+\eta}}\|_{L^{2}}}\right)dW_{z}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leq\|W\|_{[-A,A]}(2+2A+V_{-1}^{1}(K))\tilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon).

Note that
\tilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon)
is independent of
b
. Hence, for
C^{\prime}=2+2A+V_{-1}^{1}(K)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\limsup_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}(\epsilon)}\left|A_{y,h}(b)-A_{y,h}(b_{0}+\eta)\right|>\delta\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\limsup_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}\left(\|W\|_{[-A,A]}C^{\prime}\tilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon)>\delta\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\lim_{L\to\infty}\limsup_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}\left(\|W\|_{[-A,A]}C^{\prime}\tilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon)>\delta,\|W\|_{[-A,A]}\leq L\right)\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt+\lim_{L\to\infty}\limsup_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}\left(\|W\|_{[-A,A]}>L\right).\end{split}

(G.7)

In the first summand, we have for each
\delta,L
that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}\left(\|W\|_{[-A,A]}C^{\prime}\tilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon)>\delta,\|W\|_{[-A,A]}\leq L\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\limsup_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}\left(LC^{\prime}\tilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon)>\delta\right),

which equals zero, as for small enough
\epsilon
we have
\tilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon)<\frac{\delta}{LC^{\prime}}
. The latter probability does not depend on
b
and
\epsilon
. With Markov’s inequality and the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (cf.
[
27
]
, Theorem
20.12
),

\lim_{L\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\|W\|_{[-A,A]}>L\right)\leq\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\|W\|_{[-A,A]}\right]}{L}\leq\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{c\sqrt{2A}}{L}=0,

where
c>0
is some constant. Thus, the right-hand side of (
G.7
) equals zero.
The whole procedure above can be repeated for the other boundary case
b_{0}-\eta
in the same way, i.e. the left-hand side of (
G.7
) also vanishes for
b_{0}+\eta
replaced by
b_{0}-\eta
and
\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}(\epsilon)
replaced by
\mathcal{T}_{3}^{-}(\epsilon)
. Summing up, we have shown so far that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\limsup_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left|\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})dW_{s}\right|}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}}\right.\right.\\
&\hskip 170.71652pt\left.\left.-\Upsilon\left(\frac{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbbm{1}_{[-A,A]}(X_{s})ds}\right)\right)\geq r\right)\\
&\hskip 2.84544pt\leq\limsup_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}\left(\max\left\{\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left(A_{y,h}(b_{0}+\eta)-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})\right),\right.\right.\\
&\hskip 119.50148pt\left.\left.\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{-}}\left(A_{y,h}(b_{0}-\eta)-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})\right)\right\}\geq r-2\delta\right).\end{split}

(G.8)

It remains to evaluate the term
\Upsilon(\cdot)
. Here, we have

\displaystyle\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}=\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{2}\cdot\frac{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}}{\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{2}},

(G.9)

and the fraction is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{1}_{[-A,A]}(z)\frac{1}{C_{b_{0}+\eta,\sigma}}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{z}2\sigma^{-2}(b_{0}(u)+\eta)du\right)dz}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{1}_{[-A,A]}(z)\frac{1}{C_{b,\sigma}}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{z}2\sigma^{-2}b(u)du\right)dz}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)^{2}\frac{1}{C_{b,\sigma}}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{z}2\sigma^{-2}b(u)du\right)dz}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{y,h}(z)^{2}\frac{1}{C_{b_{0}+\eta,\sigma}}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{z}2\sigma^{-2}(b_{0}(u)+\eta)du\right)dz}.\end{split}

(G.10)

Clearly, the normalizing constants
C_{b,\sigma}^{-1}
and
C_{b_{0}+\eta,\sigma}^{-1}
cancel out. In both the denominator of the first factor and the nominator of the second one we have the term
\exp\left(\int_{0}^{z}2\sigma^{-2}b(u)du\right)
, which may be written as

\exp\left(\int_{0}^{z}2\sigma^{-2}(b_{0}(u)+\eta)du\right)\exp\left(\int_{0}^{z}2\sigma^{-2}(b(u)-b_{0}(u)-\eta)du\right).

On
[-A,A]
we have by assumption on
b\in H_{0}(b_{0},\eta)
that

-2\eta\leq(b(u)-b_{0}(u))-\eta\leq 0.

Remembering that we use the notation
\int_{0}^{x}f(y)dy=-\int_{x}^{0}f(y)dy
for
x<0
, we have for
z\in[-A,A]
,

e^{-4\sigma^{-2}A\eta}\leq\exp\left(\int_{0}^{z}2\sigma^{-2}(b(u)-b_{0}(u)-\eta)du\right)\leq e^{4\sigma^{-2}A\eta}.

Inserting all this in (
G.9
) and (
G.10
), it follows that

\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}=\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{2}\cdot\frac{\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}}{\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{2}}\leq e^{8\sigma^{-2}A\eta}\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{2}.

Since
\Upsilon(\cdot)
is decreasing on
[0,1]
, we get

\displaystyle\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Upsilon\left(e^{8\sigma^{-2}A\eta}\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{2}\wedge 1\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2\log\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{-2}\right)-2\log\left(e^{8\sigma^{-2}A\eta}\wedge\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{-2}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{2\log\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{-2}\right)}-\sqrt{2\log\left(e^{8\sigma^{-2}A\eta}\wedge\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{-2}\right)}

\displaystyle=\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{2}\right)-\sqrt{2\log\left(e^{8\sigma^{-2}A\eta}\wedge\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{-2}\right)}.

In the third step we used
\sqrt{a-b}\geq\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b}
for
a>b>0
together with the fact
\log(\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{-2})\geq\log(e^{8\sigma^{-2}A\eta}\wedge\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{-2})
. Hence,

\displaystyle-\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{2}\right)+\sqrt{2\log\left(e^{8\sigma^{-2}A\eta}\wedge\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{-2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq-\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{2}\right)+\sqrt{2\log\left(e^{8\sigma^{-2}A\eta}\right)}

\displaystyle=-\Upsilon\left(\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{2}\right)+4\sqrt{A\eta/\sigma^{2}}.

We thus have shown that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left(A_{y,h}(b_{0}+\eta)-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{+}}\left(A_{y,h}(b_{0}+\eta)-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{2})+4\sqrt{A\eta/\sigma^{2}}\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\sup_{\mathcal{T}}\left(A_{y,h}(b_{0}+\eta)-\Upsilon(\overline{\sigma}_{b_{0}+\eta}(y,h)^{2})\right)+4\sqrt{A\eta/\sigma^{2}}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt=U_{1}+4\sqrt{A\eta/\sigma^{2}}\end{split}

(G.11)

for
U_{1}
given in the statement of the theorem. Repeating all this arguments for
b_{0}-\eta
instead of
b_{0}+\eta
gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{\mathcal{T}_{3}^{-}}\left(A_{y,h}(b_{0}-\eta)-\Upsilon(\tilde{\sigma}_{b}(y,h)^{2})\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\sup_{\mathcal{T}}\left(A_{y,h}(b_{0}-\eta)-\Upsilon(\tilde{\sigma}_{b_{0}-\eta}(y,h)^{2})\right)+4\sqrt{A\eta/\sigma^{2}}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt=U_{2}+4\sqrt{A\eta/\sigma^{2}}.\end{split}

(G.12)

Note that these upper bounds depend no longer on
b
or
\epsilon
. In consequence, it follows from (
G.8
), the upper bounds (
G.11
) and (
G.12
) and monotonicity of measures that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\delta\searrow 0}\limsup_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}_{3}(\epsilon)}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left|\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})dW_{s}\right|}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 156.49014pt\left.\left.-\Upsilon\left(\frac{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}K_{y,h}(X_{s})^{2}ds}{\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbbm{1}_{[-A,A]}(X_{s})ds}\right)\right)\geq r-\delta\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\limsup_{\delta\searrow 0}\mathbb{P}\left(U_{1}\vee U_{2}+4\sqrt{A\eta/\sigma^{2}}\geq r-2\delta\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\mathbb{P}\left(U_{1}\vee U_{2}+4\sqrt{A\eta/\sigma^{2}}\geq r\right)

for
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
given in the statement of the theorem and the proof is complete.

G.2
Proof of Remark
4.2

In this subsection, we start with the proof of Remark
4.2
. Two lemmas used within are stated and proven thereafter. Within this subsection we abbreviate ’infinitely often’ by i.o.

We will show that for
b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)
the random variable

S_{b}=\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}}\left(\frac{\int_{-A}^{A}K_{y,h}(z)\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}dW_{z}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}-\Upsilon\left(\frac{\sigma_{b}(y,h)^{2}}{\sigma_{b,\max}^{2}}\right)\right)

has no point mass. Then in particular, both
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
have no point mass. Consequently, the same is true for their maximum because for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(U_{1}\vee U_{2}=x\right)\leq\mathbb{P}(U_{1}=x)+\mathbb{P}(U_{2}=x)=0,

which finishes the claim.

First of all,
S_{b}
has no positive atoms, which can be seen along the lines of the proof of Lemma
2
in
[
22
]
and we have to show that there is no point mass in zero, i.e.
\mathbb{P}(S_{b}>0)=1
.
By using
q_{b}\geq L_{*}
from Lemma
D.1
, we find

\displaystyle\Upsilon\left(\frac{\sigma_{b}(y,h)^{2}}{\sigma_{b,\max}^{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=2\log\left(\frac{\int_{-A}^{A}q_{b}(z)dz}{\int_{-A}^{A}K_{y,h}(z)^{2}q_{b}(z)dz}\right)\leq 2\log\left(\frac{\int_{-A}^{A}q_{b}(z)/L_{*}dz}{h\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\right)

and

S_{b}\geq\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}}\left(\frac{\int_{-A}^{A}K_{y,h}(z)\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}dW_{z}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}-\sqrt{2\log\left(\frac{L^{\prime}}{h\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\right)}\right)=:\tilde{S}_{b}(L^{\prime}),

for
L^{\prime}\geq L:=1\vee\int_{-A}^{A}q_{b}(z)/L_{*}dz
.
Now, we can write

\displaystyle X(y,h):=\int_{-A}^{A}\frac{K_{y,h}(z)\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}dW_{z}=Y_{1}(y,h)+h^{\frac{1}{4}}Y_{2}(y,h)

with

\displaystyle Y_{1}(y,h)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{h}\|K\|_{L^{2}}}\int_{-A}^{A}K_{y,h}(z)dW_{z},

\displaystyle Y_{2}(y,h)

\displaystyle:=h^{-\frac{1}{4}}\int_{-A}^{A}\left(\frac{K_{y,h}(z)\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}-\frac{K_{y,h}(z)}{\sqrt{h}\|K\|_{L^{2}}}\right)dW_{z}.

By Lemma
1
in
[
22
]
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{-A+h\leq y\leq A-h}Y_{1}(y,h)>\sqrt{2\log(L/h\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2})}\textrm{ i.o. as }h\searrow 0\right)=1.

We now apply Lemma
G.1
to this and the process
Y=Y_{2}
, which is possible due to Lemma
G.2
, and conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{-A+h\leq y\leq A-h}X(y,h)>\sqrt{2\log(L^{\prime}/h\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2})}\textrm{ i.o. as }h\searrow 0\right)=1

for some
L^{\prime}\geq L
. Following the lines of the proof of Theorem
1
in
[
22
]
we then see

\mathbb{P}(S_{b}>0)\geq\mathbb{P}(\tilde{S}_{b}(L^{\prime})>0)=1

and we conclude that
S_{b}
has no point mass in zero.
∎

Lemma G.1
.

Let
X(u)
depend on a parameter
u>0
and suppose that for all
K\geq 1
we have

\mathbb{P}\left(X(u)>\sqrt{2\log(K/u)}\textrm{ i.o. as }u\searrow 0\right)=1.

Let
Y(u)
be a family depending on
u>0
and
Y^{*}(u_{0}):=\sup_{u\leq u_{0}}Y(u)
be finite almost surely for some
u_{0}>0
. Then for every
\alpha>0
and some
K^{\prime}=K^{\prime}(\alpha)\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}\left(X(u)+u^{\alpha}Y(u)>\sqrt{2\log(K^{\prime}/u)}\textrm{ i.o. as }u\searrow 0\right)=1.

Let
\epsilon>0
. Choose
u_{0}>0
small enough and
L>0
large enough such that

A_{\epsilon}:=\left\{\sup_{u\leq u_{0}}|Y(u)|\leq L\right\}>1-\epsilon.

For this set,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(X(u)+u^{\alpha}Y(u)>\sqrt{2\log(K/u)}\textrm{ i.o. as }u\searrow 0\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\geq\mathbb{P}\left(X(u)+u^{\alpha}Y(u)>\sqrt{2\log(K/u)}\textrm{ i.o. as }u\searrow 0,A_{\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\geq\mathbb{P}\left(X(u)>\sqrt{2\log(K/u)}+u^{\alpha}L\textrm{ i.o. as }u\searrow 0,A_{\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\geq\mathbb{P}\left(X(u)>\sqrt{2\log(K/u)}+u^{\alpha}L\textrm{ i.o. as }u\searrow 0\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(A_{\epsilon}^{c}\right),

where the last step uses

\mathbb{P}(A\cap B)=\mathbb{P}(A)-\mathbb{P}(A\cap B^{c})\geq\mathbb{P}(A)-\mathbb{P}(B^{c}).

Now choose
c>1
large enough such that

\sqrt{2\log(cK/u)}\geq\sqrt{2\log(K/u)}+u^{\alpha}L

for
u<u_{0}
, which is possible since the condition is equivalent to

2\log(c)\geq 2u^{\alpha}L\sqrt{2\log(K/u)}+u^{2\alpha}L^{2}

and the right-hand side converges to zero for
u\searrow 0
. Then, for
K^{\prime}=cK
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(X(u)+u^{\alpha}Y(u)>\sqrt{2\log(K/u)}\textrm{ i.o. as }u\searrow 0\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\geq\mathbb{P}\left(X(u)>\sqrt{2\log(K^{\prime}/u)}\textrm{ i.o. as }u\searrow 0\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(A_{\epsilon}^{c}\right).

The first probability equals one by assumption and the claim follows by letting
\epsilon\searrow 0
.
∎

Lemma G.2
.

Define

Y(y,h):=h^{-\frac{1}{4}}\int_{-A}^{A}\frac{K_{y,h}(z)}{\sqrt{h}\|K\|_{L^{2}}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}\sqrt{h}\|K\|}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}-1\right)dW_{z}.

Then there exists
0<h_{0}\leq A
such that the random variable

Y(h_{0}):=\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}:h\leq h_{0}}Y(y,h)

is finite almost surely.

Define the functions

f_{y,h}^{1}(z):=\frac{K_{y,h}(z)}{h^{3/4}\|K\|_{L^{2}}}\hskip 10.00002pt\textrm{ and }\hskip 10.00002ptf_{y,h}^{2}(z):=\frac{\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}\sqrt{h}\|K\|_{L^{2}}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}-1.

Then
f_{y,h}^{1}
is of bounded variation as this holds for
K
, and
f_{y,h}^{2}
is of bounded variation because
q_{b}
is differentiable with bounded derivative (cf.
[
46
]
, Example
6.23
(b) and Lemma
D.1
). Consequently,
f_{y,h}^{1}\cdot f_{y,h}^{2}
is of bounded variation as a product of two such functions (cf.
[
46
]
, Theorem
6.24
). Then by (
G.5
),

|Y(y,h)|\leq\|W\|_{[-A,A]}\left(2\|f_{y,h}^{1}\cdot f_{y,h}^{2}\|_{\infty}+V_{-A}^{A}\left(f_{y,h}^{1}\cdot f_{y,h}^{2}\right)\right),

where
V_{a}^{b}(g)
denotes the variation of
g
on the interval
[a,b]
. Because the random variable
\|W\|_{[-A,A]}
is finite almost surely, the claim follows if we show that

(1)

\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}:h\leq h_{0}}\|f_{y,h}^{1}\cdot f_{y,h}^{2}\|_{\infty}<\infty
, and

(2)

\sup_{(y,h)\in\mathcal{T}:h\leq h_{0}}V_{-A}^{A}\left(f_{y,h}^{1}\cdot f_{y,h}^{2}\right)<\infty

for some
0<h_{0}\leq A
. Subsequently, we will show these two items separately.

Item (1).
We choose
h_{0}
small enough such that
4L^{*}h_{0}<L_{*}/2
. We have,

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}\sqrt{h}\|K\|_{L^{2}}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}=\sqrt{\frac{(q_{b}(y)+q_{b}(z)-q_{b}(y))h\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{q_{b}(y)h\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{-A}^{A}K_{y,h}(z)^{2}(q_{b}(z)-q_{b}(y))dz}}.

Using
|q_{b}(z)-q_{b}(y)|\leq L^{*}h
for
|z-y|\leq h
by Lemma
D.1
, we give an upper and lower bound for the last expression. We start with the upper bound

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{q_{b}(y)h\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+L^{*}\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}h^{2}}{q_{b}(y)h\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-L^{*}\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}h^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{q_{b}(y)+L^{*}h}{q_{b}(y)-L^{*}h}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{1+\frac{q_{b}(y)+L^{*}h-q_{b}(y)+L^{*}h}{q_{b}(y)-L^{*}h}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{1+4hL^{*}/L_{*}},

where we used in the last line that
q_{b}(y)-L^{*}h\geq L_{*}-L^{*}h\geq L_{*}/2
by choice of
h\leq h_{0}
. Similiarly, we find the lower bound

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{q_{b}(y)h\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-L^{*}\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}h^{2}}{q_{b}(y)h\|K\|_{L^{2}}+L^{*}\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{2}h^{2}}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{1-4hL^{*}/L_{*}}.

In consequence, we conclude by
\left(1-\sqrt{1+x}\right)^{2}\leq x^{2}
for
x\geq-1
that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}\sqrt{h}\|K\|_{L^{2}}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}-1\right)^{2}\leq\max_{v=\pm 1}\left(\sqrt{1+4vhL^{*}/L_{*}}-1\right)^{2}\leq c^{2}h^{2}

(G.13)

with
c:=4L^{*}/L_{*}
. This direcly implies that
\|f_{y,h}^{2}\|_{\infty}\leq ch
, in particular this bound is independent of
y
. Bounding
f_{y,h}^{1}
is straightforward, as
\|K\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and we thus have

\|f_{y,h}^{1}\cdot f_{y,h}^{2}\|_{\infty}\leq h^{-3/4}\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{-1}ch=c\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{-1}h^{1/4}\leq c\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{-1}A^{1/4},

which is independent of
y
and
h
.

Item (2).
For the variation term we have (cf.
[
46
]
, proof of Theorem
6.24
)

\displaystyle V_{-A}^{A}\left(f_{y,h}^{1}\cdot f_{y,h}^{2}\right)\leq\|f_{y,h}^{1}\|_{\infty}V_{y-h}^{y+h}\left(f_{y,h}^{2}\right)+\|f_{y,h}^{2}\|_{\infty}V_{y-h}^{y+h}\left(f_{y,h}^{1}\right).

(G.14)

As seen in item (1), we have
\|f_{y,h}^{1}\|_{\infty}\leq h^{-3/4}\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{-1}
and
\|f_{y,h}^{2}\|_{\infty}\leq ch
for
h\leq h_{0}<L_{*}/(8L^{*})
. Moreover,

V_{y-h}^{y+h}\left(f_{y,h}^{1}\right)=h^{-3/4}\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{-1}V_{y-h}^{y+h}(K_{y,h})=h^{-3/4}\|K\|_{L^{2}}^{-1}V_{-1}^{1}(K)

and

V_{y-h}^{y+h}\left(f_{y,h}^{2}\right)=\frac{\sqrt{h}\|K\|_{L^{2}}}{\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}}V_{y-h}^{y+h}\left(\sqrt{q_{b}}\right).

For differentiable
g
we have
V_{a}^{b}(g)=\int_{a}^{b}|g^{\prime}(z)|dz
(cf.
[
46
]
, Theorem
6.35
). Using
q_{b}^{\prime}=2\sigma^{-2}bq_{b}
, the at most linear growth condition on
b
and the uniform bound
q_{b}\leq L^{*}
from Lemma
D.1
,

\displaystyle V_{y-h}^{y+h}\left(\sqrt{q_{b}}\right)=\sigma^{-2}\int_{y-h}^{y+h}|b(z)|\sqrt{q_{b}(z)}dz\leq 2h\sigma^{-2}C(1+A)\sqrt{L^{*}}.

Using that
\|K_{y,h}\sqrt{q_{b}}\|_{L^{2}}\geq\sqrt{hL_{*}}\|K\|_{L_{2}}
with the lower bound of Lemma
D.1
, we then have

V_{y-h}^{y+h}\left(f_{y,h}^{2}\right)\leq 2h\sigma^{-2}C(1+A)\sqrt{L^{*}/L_{*}}.
