\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left|b_{n_{k}}(X_{s}^{H_{n_{k}},b_{n_{k}}})-b_{n_{k}}(X_{s}^{H,b})\right|+\left|b_{n_{k}}(X_{s}^{H,b})-b(X_{s}^{H,b})\right|ds

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\sigma\left|W_{t}^{H_{n_{k}}}-W_{t}^{H}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq L\int_{0}^{t}\left|X_{s}^{H_{n_{k}},b_{n_{k}}}-X_{s}^{H,b}\right|ds+t\|b_{n_{k}}-b\|_{K}+\sigma\left|W_{t}^{H_{n_{k}}}-W_{t}^{H}\right|.

By Gronwall’s lemma (cf.
(2.11)
in
[
28
]
, Chapter
5
with corresponding proof on p. 387–388) we get

\displaystyle\left|X_{t}^{H_{n_{k}},b_{n_{k}}}-X_{t}^{H,b}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(t\|b_{n_{k}}-b\|_{K}+\sigma\left|W_{t}^{H_{n_{k}}}-W_{t}^{H}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+L\int_{0}^{t}\left(s\|b_{n_{k}}-b\|_{K}+\sigma\left|W_{s}^{H_{n_{k}}}-W_{s}^{H}\right|\right)e^{L(t-s)}ds

and in consequence

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|X_{t}^{H_{n_{k}},b_{n_{k}}}-X_{t}^{H,b}\right\|_{[0,T]}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\left(T\|b_{n_{k}}-b\|_{K}+\sigma\left\|W^{H_{n_{k}}}-W^{H}\right\|_{[0,T]}\right)e^{LT}.\end{split}

(I.14)

Now, we distinguish cases. By Lemma
I.9
we either have

(i)

H_{n_{k}}=H
for all
k\geq k_{0}
for some
k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
, or

(ii)

H=\frac{1}{2}
.

First, suppose (i) holds true. Then for
k\geq k_{0}
, (
I.14
) simplifies to

\left\|X_{t}^{H_{n_{k}},b_{n_{k}}}-X_{t}^{H,b}\right\|_{[0,T]}\leq T\|b_{n_{k}}-b\|_{K}\cdot e^{LT},

which converges to zero as
k\to\infty
. Hence, the right-hand side of (
I.13
) vanishes in the limit
k\to\infty
by dominated convergence and we have

\mathbb{P}^{X^{H_{n_{k}},b_{n_{k}}}}\Rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{X^{H,b}}.

Assume (ii) holds true. Then
\|W^{H_{n_{k}}}-W^{H}\|_{[0,T]}
converges to zero in probability by Proposition
6.2
. Therefore, we find a subsequence
(H_{n_{k_{l}}})_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
with

\|W^{H_{n_{k_{l}}}}-W^{H}\|_{[0,T]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle l\to\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}0\hskip 10.00002pta.s.

(cf. Lemma
5.2
in
[
27
]
). Then we repeat the above arguments with
n_{k}
replaced by
n_{k_{l}}
, in particular (
I.14
) tends to zero for
l\to\infty
almost surely and consequently the right-hand side in (
I.13
) converges to zero, which implies

\mathbb{P}^{X^{H_{n_{k_{l}}},b_{n_{k_{l}}}}}\Rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{X^{H,b}}.

In either way, we have shown that any sequence
\{\mathbb{P}^{X^{H_{n},b_{n}}}:n\in\mathbb{N}\}
contains a weakly convergent subsequence and hence the family

\left\{\mathbb{P}^{X^{H,b}}:b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)\cap\mathcal{H}(1,L),H\in\{h_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\}\right\}

is relatively compact and by Prohorov’s theorem (
[
8
]
, Theorem
6.2
) it is tight.
∎

Let
D_{c,T}:=\{f\in\mathcal{C}([0,T])\mid-c,c\in\textrm{im}(f)\}
be given as in (
I.12
). Unless stated otherwise, we denote
X^{H,b}=(X_{t}^{H,b})_{t\leq T}
and
X^{b}=(X_{t}^{b})_{t\leq T}
. First, we note that for any
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(X^{H,b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(X^{b}\in D_{A+\epsilon^{\prime},T}\textrm{ and }\|X^{H,b}-X^{b}\|_{[0,T]}<\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(X^{b}\in D_{A+\epsilon^{\prime},T}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\|X^{H,b}-X^{b}\|_{[0,T]}>\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{2}\right)

and in consequence

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\inf_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(X^{H,b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\geq\inf_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(X^{b}\in D_{A+\epsilon^{\prime},T}\right)-\sup_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(\|X^{H,b}-X^{b}\|_{[0,T]}>\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{2}\right),\end{split}

(I.15)

where
\inf_{b}
and
\sup_{b}
are taken over
\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)\cap\mathcal{H}(1,L)
. By Lemma
I.7
we find
T_{0}
and
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
such that the first term is bounded away from zero for all
T\geq T_{0}
and consequently by Proposition
6.2

\displaystyle\liminf_{H\to\frac{1}{2}}\inf_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(X^{H,b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\right)>0

(I.16)

for
T\geq T_{0}
. As

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|>\epsilon\ \right|X^{H,b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|>\epsilon\textrm{ and }X^{H,b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\right)}{\mathbb{P}\left(X^{H,b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\right)},

the first claim of the proposition follows from (
I.16
) once we have shown that

\lim_{H\to\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|>\epsilon\textrm{ and }X^{H,b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\right)=0,

which is equivalent to showing that for any sequence
(H_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
H_{n}\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H_{n},b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|>\epsilon\textrm{ and }X^{H_{n},b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\right)=0.

(I.17)

To see this, let
(H_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be such a sequence and
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}>0
. By Lemma
I.11
there exist compact sets
K_{1}=K_{1}((H_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}})
and
K_{2}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(X^{H_{n},b}\in K_{1}^{c}\right)\leq\epsilon^{\prime\prime}\ \textrm{ and }\ \sup_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(X^{b}\in K_{2}^{c}\right)\leq\epsilon^{\prime\prime}.

(I.18)

With the compact set
K=K_{1}\cup K_{2}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H_{n},b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|>\epsilon\textrm{ and }X^{H_{n},b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76228pt\leq\sup_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H_{n},b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|>\epsilon\textrm{ and }X^{H_{n},b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\cap K\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\sup_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{X^{H_{n},b},X^{b}\in K\right\}^{c}\right).

By Lemma
I.8
, the set
D_{A,T}\cap K
is compact as a closed subset of a compact set. By Theorem
6.1
, the map
\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}
is continuous on
D_{A,T}
and hence, it is uniformly continuous on the compact set
D_{A,T}\cap K
. Therefore, Lemma
I.6
is applicable on this set and the limit
\lim_{n\to\infty}
of the first term is zero by Proposition
6.2
. By the union bound and (
I.18
), the second term is bounded by
2\epsilon^{\prime\prime}
and (
I.17
) follows as
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}>0
was arbitrary.

For the second claim of the theorem, we start with inequality (
I.15
). As

\liminf_{T\to\infty}\inf_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(X^{b}\in D_{A+\epsilon^{\prime},T}\right)=1

by Lemma
I.7
for a proper choice of
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
, and

\lim_{H\to\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(\|X^{H,b}-X^{b}\|_{[0,T]}>\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{2}\right)=0

by Proposition
6.2
, we directly get from (
I.15
) that

\liminf_{T\to\infty}\liminf_{H\to\frac{1}{2}}\inf_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(X^{H,b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\right)=1.

For the other order of
\liminf
we note that

\left\{(X_{t}^{H,b})_{t\leq T_{0}},(X_{t}^{b})_{t\leq T_{0}}\in D_{A,T}\right\}\subset\left\{(X_{t}^{H,b})_{t\leq T},(X_{t}^{b})_{t\leq T}\in D_{A,T}\right\}

for
T_{0}\leq T
and hence for any
T_{0}>0

\liminf_{T\to\infty}\inf_{b}\mathbb{P}\left(X^{H,b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\right)\geq\inf_{b}\mathbb{P}\left((X_{t}^{H,b})_{t\leq T_{0}},(X_{t}^{b})_{t\leq T_{0}}\in D_{A,T_{0}}\right).

Let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrary. Choose
T_{0}
large enough and
\delta>0
such that
\inf_{b}\mathbb{P}\left((X_{t}^{b})_{t\leq T_{0}}\in D_{A+\delta,T_{0}}\right)\geq 1-\epsilon
, which is possible by Lemma
I.7
. Then again by (
I.15
) we find

\displaystyle\liminf_{H\to\frac{1}{2}}\liminf_{T\to\infty}\inf_{b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)\cap\mathcal{H}(1,L)}\mathbb{P}\left(X^{H,b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\geq\liminf_{H\to\frac{1}{2}}\inf_{b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)\cap\mathcal{H}(1,L)}\mathbb{P}\left((X_{t}^{H,b})_{t\leq T_{0}},(X_{t}^{b})_{t\leq T_{0}}\in D_{A,T_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\geq\inf_{b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)\cap\mathcal{H}(1,L)}\mathbb{P}\left((X_{t}^{b})_{t\leq T_{0}}\in D_{A+\delta,T_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\limsup_{H\to\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)\cap\mathcal{H}(1,L)}\mathbb{P}\left(\|X^{H,b}-X^{b}\|_{[0,T_{0}]}>\delta/2\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\geq 1-\epsilon,

where we applied Proposition
6.2
in the last step. The assertion follows by taking
\epsilon\searrow 0
.
∎

As a first step, for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})>\kappa\right)&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})>\kappa,\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|\leq\epsilon\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|>\epsilon\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})>\kappa-\epsilon\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|>\epsilon\right).\end{split}

(I.19)

To estimate the second summand, we denote
B_{H,b}:=\{X^{H,b},X^{b}\in D_{A,T}\}
and apply the law of total probability and get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|>\epsilon\ \right|B_{H,b}\right)\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(B_{H,b}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|>\epsilon\ \right|B_{H,b}^{c}\right)\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(B_{H,b}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|>\epsilon\ \right|B_{H,b}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(B_{H,b}^{c}\right).

By Theorem
6.3
,

\limsup_{T\to\infty}\limsup_{H\to\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{b\in H_{0}(b_{0},\eta)\cap\mathcal{H}(1,L)}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})-\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})\right|>\epsilon\right)=0.

In conclusion, (
I.19
) yields for any
\epsilon>0
with
\kappa-\epsilon>\kappa_{\eta,\alpha}

\displaystyle\limsup_{T\to\infty}\limsup_{H\to\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{b\in H_{0}(b_{0},\eta)\cap\mathcal{H}(1,L)}\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{H,b})>\kappa\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}(b_{0},\eta)\cap\mathcal{H}(1,L)}\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})>\kappa-\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\limsup_{T\to\infty}\sup_{b\in H_{0}(b_{0},\eta)\cap\mathcal{H}(1,L)}\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}(X^{b})>\kappa_{\eta,\alpha}\right)\leq\alpha.

The last step used that
T_{T}^{\eta}
is uniformly asymptotically of level
\alpha
, see (
4.8
), in combination with (
F.18
) that implies that the restriction to
\mathcal{T}_{T}
in the definition of
\tilde{T}_{T}^{\eta}
compared to all of
\mathcal{T}
is negligible in the limit.∎

I.4
Proof of Theorem
6.5

First, we present the following result that is used later in our proof.

Lemma I.12
(
[
48
]
, Lemma
8
).

Let
T>0
and
\frac{1}{2}<\beta<H<1
. Then we have the following moment estimate for any
k\geq 1
:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sup_{s\neq r\in[0,T]}\frac{|W_{s}^{H}-W_{r}^{H}|}{|s-r|^{\beta}}\right)^{2k}\right]\leq 32^{k}(2T)^{2k(H-\beta)}\frac{(2k)!}{k!}.

Next, we establish
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
-convergence of the exponent of the density given by the fractional Girsanov theorem
I.3
as
H\to\frac{1}{2}
.

Lemma I.13
.

Let
b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)\cap\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)
,
L>0
, and
g:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a function with
\|g\|_{\infty}<\infty
that is Lipschitz continuous with constant
\|g\|_{L}
. Define

v_{H}^{g}(s):=K_{H}^{-1}\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}g(X_{u}^{H,b})du\right)(s).

Then we have

\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)dW_{s}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H\to\frac{1}{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P})}\int_{0}^{T}g(X_{s}^{b})dW_{s}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}g(X_{s}^{b})^{2}ds.

As the representation of
v_{H}^{g}
in terms of fractional integrals and derivatives is different for
H<\frac{1}{2}
and
H>\frac{1}{2}
we distinguish between those two cases.

Case 1:
H<\frac{1}{2}
. First of all, we show that
v_{H}^{g}(s)
is bounded on
[0,T]
uniformly in
H<\frac{1}{2}
. This follows by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|v_{H}^{g}(s)\right|&=\left|s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}I_{0+}^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\left((\cdot)^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})\right)(s)\right|\\
&=s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-H)}\left|\int_{0}^{s}\frac{u^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{u}^{H,b})}{(s-u)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}du\right|\\
&\leq s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\|g\|_{\infty}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-H)}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{u^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{(s-u)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}du\\
&=s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\|g\|_{\infty}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-H)}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{3}{2}-H)}{\Gamma(2-2H)}s^{1-2H}\\
&=\frac{\|g\|_{\infty}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-H)}\frac{\left(\frac{1}{2}-H\right)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-H)}{\Gamma(2-2H)}s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\\
&\leq\|g\|_{\infty}\sqrt{T},\end{split}

(I.20)

where we evaluated the integral in the forth step according to (
I.6
). Furthermore, we used in the fifth stept that
x\Gamma(x)=\Gamma(x+1)
for
x>0
and in the last one that
s\in[0,T]
and
(\frac{1}{2}-H)(\Gamma(2-2H)^{-1}\leq 1
. This last property follows from
\Gamma(x)\geq\frac{1}{2}
for
x\in[1,2]
which can be seen in the following way: For all those
x
we have

\displaystyle\Gamma(x)=\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{x-1}e^{-t}dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}t^{x-1}e^{-t}dt+\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{x-1}e^{-t}dt

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{1}te^{-t}dt+\int_{1}^{\infty}e^{-t}dt

\displaystyle=\left[-te^{-t}\right]_{0}^{1}+\left[-e^{-t}\right]_{0}^{1}+\left[-e^{-t}\right]_{1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle=1-e^{-1}

and now we simply use that
e>2
.

To proceed with the proof, we will show
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
-convergence of the stochastic integral and the Lebesgue integral separately. For the stochastic integrals we have by Itô’s isometry

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)dW_{s}-\int_{0}^{T}g(X_{s}^{b})dW_{s}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)-g(X_{s}^{b})dW_{s}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(v_{H}^{g}(s)-g(X_{s}^{b})\right)^{2}ds\right].

By the uniform in
H
boundedness of
v_{H}^{g}
and
g
we get uniform integrability of the inner integral process (indexed in
H
) and are done if we can show that it converges to zero in probability for
H\nearrow\frac{1}{2}
(cf.
[
27
]
, Theorem
5.12
). This holds true, if
\|v_{H}^{g}-g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}\rightarrow 0
in probability.

Using again uniform integrability, we may show
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
-convergence of the Lebesgue integral if we establish that

\displaystyle\|v_{H}^{g}\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}^{2}=\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle H\nearrow\frac{1}{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\ \int_{0}^{T}g(X_{s}^{b})^{2}ds=\|g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}^{2}

(I.21)

in probability. By the reverse triangle inequality we have

\displaystyle\left|\|v_{H}^{g}\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}-\|g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}\right|\leq\|v_{H}^{g}-g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\|_{L^{2}([0,T])},

(I.22)

so convergence of
\|v_{H}^{g}-g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}
to zero in probability suffices to show
\|v_{H}^{g}\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}\|g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}
. By taking squares and applying the continuous mapping theorem we conclude that convergence in probability holds in (
I.21
).
Thus, we are finished for both the Itô and the Lebesgue integral, if we show
\|v_{H}^{g}-g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0
. To this aim, we split in the following way:

\displaystyle v_{H}^{g}(s)-g(X_{s}^{b})

\displaystyle=s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}I_{0+}^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\left((\cdot)^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})\right)(s)-g(X_{s}^{b})

\displaystyle=\left(I_{0+}^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\left((\cdot)^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})\right)(s)-I_{0+}^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\left(g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\right)(s)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left(I_{0+}^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\left(g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\right)(s)-g(X_{s}^{b})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left(s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}-1\right)I_{0+}^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\left((\cdot)^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})\right)(s).

By Minkowski’s inequality for the
L^{2}([0,T])
-norm, it is enough to show convergence in probability to zero for the
L^{2}([0,T])
-norm of each summand separately.

(1)

For the first summand, we use the
L^{2}([0,T])
-estimate of equation
(2.72)
in
[
47
]
to get

\displaystyle\left\|I_{0+}^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\left((\cdot)^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})\right)-I_{0+}^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\left(g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\right)\right\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\frac{T^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{(\frac{1}{2}-H)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-H)}\left\|(\cdot)^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})-g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\right\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}.

As
x\Gamma(x)=\Gamma(x+1)
for
x>0
, we have for
H<\frac{1}{2}
,

\frac{T^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{(\frac{1}{2}-H)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-H)}=\frac{T^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{\Gamma(\frac{3}{2}-H)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H\nearrow\frac{1}{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\ 1.

Next, we evaluate the squared
L^{2}([0,T])
-norm:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left(u^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{u}^{H,b})-g(X_{u}^{b})\right)^{2}du

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt=\int_{0}^{T}\left(u^{\frac{1}{2}-H}(g(X_{u}^{H,b})-g(X_{u}^{b}))-g(X_{u}^{b})\left(1-u^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\right)\right)^{2}du

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt\leq 2\int_{0}^{T}u^{1-2H}(g(X_{u}^{H,b})-g(X_{u}^{b}))^{2}du+2\int_{0}^{T}g(X_{u}^{b})^{2}\left(u^{\frac{1}{2}-H}-1\right)^{2}du.

The second summand is bounded by

\displaystyle 2\|g\|_{\infty}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\left(u^{\frac{1}{2}-H}-1\right)^{2}du

\displaystyle=2\|g\|_{\infty}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\left(u^{1-2H}-2u^{\frac{1}{2}-H}+1\right)du

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt=2\|g\|_{\infty}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2-2H}T^{2-2H}-\frac{2}{\frac{3}{2}-H}T^{\frac{3}{2}-H}+T\right),

which converges to zero for
H\nearrow\frac{1}{2}
. The first summand, on the other hand, can be bounded by

\displaystyle 2\left\|g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})-g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\right\|_{[0,T]}\int_{0}^{T}u^{1-2H}du

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\leq 2\|g\|_{L}\|X_{\cdot}^{H,b}-X_{\cdot}^{b}\|_{[0,T]}\cdot\frac{1}{2-2H}T^{2-2H}.

This term converges to zero in probability for
H\nearrow\frac{1}{2}
because the term
\|X_{\cdot}^{H,b}-X_{\cdot}^{b}\|_{[0,T]}
does by Proposition
6.2
and the other factors are bounded.

(2)

By Theorem
2.6
in
[
47
]
, we simply have for all
\omega\in\Omega
,

\left\|I_{0+}^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\left(g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\right)-g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\right\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}\ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle H\nearrow\frac{1}{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\ 0.

(3)

As in (2), we argue
\omega
-wise. We repeat the argument used for bounding
v_{H}^{b}
in (
I.20
) without the factor
s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}
and immediately see that

\left|I_{0+}^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\left((\cdot)^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})\right)(s)\right|\leq\|g\|_{\infty}T.

Hence,

\displaystyle\left\|\left((\cdot)^{H-\frac{1}{2}}-1\right)I_{0+}^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\left((\cdot)^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})\right)\right\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\|g\|_{\infty}T\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left(s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}-1\right)^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\|g\|_{\infty}T\left(\frac{T^{2H}}{2H}-2\frac{2T^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}{2H+1}+T\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle H\nearrow\frac{1}{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\ 0.

This finishes the proof in the case
H<\frac{1}{2}
.

Case 2:
H>\frac{1}{2}
. The idea is again to show
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
-convergence of the Itô integral and the Lebesgue integral separately. For the stochastic integral we have by Itô’s isometry

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)dW_{s}-\int_{0}^{T}g(X_{s}^{b})dW_{s}\right)^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(v_{H}^{g}(s)-g(X_{s}^{b})\right)^{2}ds\right],

but this time we use (
I.4
) to represent
v_{H}^{g}
as

\displaystyle v_{H}^{g}(s)

\displaystyle=s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}D_{0+}^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\left((\cdot)^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})\right)(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}\left(\frac{s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{s}^{H,b})}{s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\left.+\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)\int_{0}^{s}\frac{s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{s}^{H,b})-r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{r}^{H,b})}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}dr\right).

Using repeatedly
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
, we see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(v_{H}^{g}(s)-g(X_{s}^{b})\right)^{2}ds\right]&\leq 2A_{H}+2B_{H}\\
&\leq 2A_{H}+4B_{H}^{1}+4B_{H}^{2},\end{split}

(I.23)

where

\displaystyle A_{H}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(\frac{s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{s}^{H,b})}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}-g(X_{s}^{b})\right)^{2}ds\right],

\displaystyle B_{H}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(\frac{\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{s}^{H,b})-r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}g(X_{r}^{H,b})}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}dr\right)^{2}ds\right],

and we subsequently splitted
B_{H}
in a similar way as in the proof of Theorem
3
in
[
40
]
into

\displaystyle B_{H}^{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(\frac{\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}g(X_{s}^{H,b})\int_{0}^{s}\frac{s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}-r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}dr\right)^{2}ds\right],

\displaystyle B_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(\frac{\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{g(X_{s}^{H,b})-g(X_{r}^{H,b})}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}dr\right)^{2}ds\right].

In the following we will show for each of
A_{H},B_{H}^{1}
and
B_{H}^{2}
that it vanishes in the limit
H\searrow\frac{1}{2}
.

A_{H}
:
For this term, we start by

\displaystyle A_{H}=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left(\frac{s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}-1\right)g(X_{s}^{H,b})+\left(g(X_{s}^{H,b})-g(X_{s}^{b})\right)\right)^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(\frac{s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}-1\right)^{2}g(X_{s}^{H,b})^{2}ds+\int_{0}^{T}\left(g(X_{s}^{H,b})-g(X_{s}^{b})\right)^{2}ds\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq 2\|g\|_{\infty}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\frac{s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}-1\right)^{2}ds+T\mathbb{E}\left[\|g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})-g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\|_{[0,T]}^{2}\right].

For the first summand, we evaluate the integral and get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left(\frac{s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}-1\right)^{2}ds

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\int_{0}^{T}\frac{s^{1-2H}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)^{2}}-\frac{2s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}+1\ ds

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\frac{T^{2-2H}}{(2-2H)\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)^{2}}-\frac{2T^{\frac{3}{2}-H}}{\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}+T\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H\searrow\frac{1}{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

For the second summand, we note that the family of random variables

\left\{\|g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})-g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\|_{[0,T]}^{2},\frac{1}{2}\leq H<1\right\}

is uniformly integrable as all of them are bounded by
4T\|g\|_{\infty}^{2}
. Thus, it suffices to establish convergence in probability to zero of this term instead (cf.
[
27
]
, Theorem
5.12
). But this follows easily by the Lipschitz condition on
g
and Proposition
6.2
as

\|g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})-g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\|_{[0,T]}^{2}\leq\|g\|_{L}^{2}\|X_{\cdot}^{H,b}-X_{\cdot}^{b}\|_{[0,T]}^{2}.

In conclusion, we have shown so far that
A_{H}\rightarrow 0
for
H\searrow\frac{1}{2}
.

B_{H}^{1}
:
Bounding
B_{H}^{1}
essentially relies on finding the value of the inner integral. For this, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\frac{s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}-r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}dr=\left(\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)^{2}}{\Gamma(2-2H)}-1\right)\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{-1}s^{1-2H}

(I.24)

almost everywhere, which can be seen by evaluating the fractional derivative
D_{0+}^{H-1/2}\left((\cdot)^{1/2-H}\right)
in two ways. First, by
direct evaluation via (
I.5
),

D_{0+}^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\left((\cdot)^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\right)(s)=\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}{\Gamma(2-2H)}s^{1-2H}.

On the other hand,
s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}\in I_{0+}^{H-1/2}(L^{1}([0,T])
as
\int_{0}^{T}u^{1-2H}du<\infty
and

I_{0+}^{H-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{3}{2}-H)}{\Gamma(2-2H)}(\cdot)^{1-2H}\right)(s)=s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}

by (
I.6
).
Then, by the Weyl representation (
I.4
),
D_{0+}^{H-1/2}\left((\cdot)^{1/2-H}\right)
is given by

\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}\left(s^{1-2H}+\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)\int_{0}^{s}\frac{s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}-r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}dr\right)\hskip 10.00002pta.e.

Combining these two results gives (
I.24
) after rearranging. With this identity holding almost everywhere,

\displaystyle B_{H}^{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)^{2}}\|g\|_{\infty}^{2}\left(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{3}{2}-H)^{2}}{\Gamma(2-2H)}-1\right)^{2}\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{-2}\int_{0}^{T}s^{2H-1}s^{2-4H}ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2-2H)\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)^{2}}\left(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{3}{2}-H)^{2}}{\Gamma(2-2H)}-1\right)^{2}\|g\|_{\infty}^{2}T^{2-2H}

which tends to zero for
H\searrow\frac{1}{2}
.

B_{H}^{2}
:
Here, it is crucial to find a bound for the inner integral. For this, we first use the Lipschitz property of
g
and get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{s}\frac{g(X_{s}^{H,b})-g(X_{r}^{H,b})}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}dr\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{s}\frac{|g(X_{s}^{H,b})-g(X_{r}^{H,b})|}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}dr

\displaystyle\leq\|g\|_{L}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{|X_{s}^{H,b}-X_{r}^{H,b}|}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}dr.

By the definition of
X^{H,b}
we furthermore have for
s\geq r
,

X_{s}^{H,b}-X_{r}^{H,b}=\int_{r}^{s}b(X_{u}^{H,b})du+\sigma(W_{s}^{H}-W_{r}^{H}).

By the at most linear growth condition on
b
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{r}^{s}b(X_{u}^{H,b})du\right|

\displaystyle\leq|s-r|\cdot\|b(X_{\cdot}^{H,b})\|_{[0,T]}

\displaystyle\leq C|s-r|\left(1+\|X^{H,b}\|_{[0,T]}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C|s-r|\left(1+CT+\|W^{H}\|_{[0,T]}\right)e^{CT},

where the last step used Lemma
1
in
[
52
]
. To proceed, we choose a number
0<\epsilon_{H}<\min\{H-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}\}

and estimate (note that
W_{0}=0
),

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|W_{t}^{H}|

\displaystyle=\sup_{0<t\leq T}\frac{|W_{t}^{H}|}{t^{H-\epsilon_{H}}}t^{H-\epsilon_{H}}

\displaystyle\leq T^{H-\epsilon_{H}}\sup_{0<t\leq T}\frac{|W_{t}^{H}|}{t^{H-\epsilon_{H}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(T\vee T^{\frac{1}{4}}\right)\sup_{s\neq t\in[0,T]}\frac{|W_{t}^{H}-W_{s}^{H}|}{|t-s|^{H-\epsilon_{H}}}.

Introducing the random variable

\displaystyle G_{H}:=\sup_{s\neq r\in[0,T]}\frac{|W_{s}^{H}-W_{r}^{H}|}{|s-r|^{H-\epsilon_{H}}},

(I.25)

we have for a constant
C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}(T)>0
not depending on
H
,

|X_{s}^{H,b}-X_{r}^{H,b}|\leq C^{\prime}|s-r|\left(1+G_{H}\right)+\sigma|W_{s}^{H}-W_{r}^{H}|.

Using
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
once again, we find
B_{H}^{2}\leq 2B_{H}^{2,1}+2B_{H}^{2,2}
with

\displaystyle B_{H}^{2,1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\|g\|_{L}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\frac{\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}C^{\prime}(1+G_{H})\int_{0}^{s}\frac{(s-r)r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}dr\right)^{2}ds\right],

\displaystyle B_{H}^{2,2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\|g\|_{L}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\frac{\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)s^{H-\frac{1}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}\sigma\int_{0}^{s}\frac{|W_{s}^{H}-W_{r}^{H}|}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}dr\right)^{2}ds\right].

For
B_{H}^{2,1}
we first evaluate the inner integral and get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\frac{(s-r)}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}dr

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{s}\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{(s-r)^{-\frac{1}{2}+H}}dr=\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}{\Gamma\left(3-2H\right)}s^{2-2H}

using (
I.6
). This gives the bound

\displaystyle B_{H}^{2,1}

\displaystyle\leq(C^{\prime})^{2}\|g\|_{L}^{2}\frac{\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}}{\Gamma(3-2H)^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[(1+G_{H})^{2}\right]\int_{0}^{T}s^{3-2H}ds

\displaystyle=(C^{\prime})^{2}\|g\|_{L}^{2}\frac{\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}}{\Gamma(3-2H)^{2}}\frac{1}{4-2H}T^{4-2H}\mathbb{E}\left[(1+G_{H})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(C^{\prime})^{2}\|g\|_{L}^{2}\frac{\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}}{\Gamma(3-2H)^{2}}\frac{1}{4-2H}T^{4-2H}\left(2+128(2T)^{\epsilon_{H}}\right),

where we used Lemma
I.12
in the last step. An application of this lemma is possible since
H>H-\epsilon_{H}>\frac{1}{2}
. It follows that
B_{H}^{2,1}\rightarrow 0
for
H\searrow\frac{1}{2}
.

A similar procedure will be applied for
B_{H}^{2,2}
. For
0<\epsilon_{H}<\min\{H-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}\}
, we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\sigma|W_{s}^{H}-W_{r}^{H}|}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+H}}r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}dr

\displaystyle\leq\sigma G_{H}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{(s-r)^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon_{H}}}dr

\displaystyle=\sigma G_{H}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}{\Gamma(2-H-\epsilon_{H})}s^{1-H-\epsilon_{H}}

with (
I.6
) and the random variable
G_{H}
given in (
I.25
).
This gives rise to the bound

\displaystyle B_{H}^{2,2}\leq\|g\|_{L}^{2}\frac{\sigma^{2}\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}}{\Gamma(2-H-\epsilon_{H})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[G_{H}^{2}\right]\int_{0}^{T}s^{1-2\epsilon_{H}}ds.

Again, we can apply Lemma
I.12
to bound the expectation whereas it is simple to evaluate the integral. This together gives

B_{H}^{2,2}\leq\|g\|_{L}^{2}\frac{\sigma^{2}\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}}{\Gamma(2-H-\epsilon_{H})^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2-2\epsilon_{H}}T^{2-2\epsilon_{H}}\right)\left(64(2T)^{\epsilon_{H}}\right)

and it now can be seen that
B_{H}^{2,2}\rightarrow 0
for
H\searrow\frac{1}{2}
. This establishes the convergence of the Itô integrals of the statement as the right-hand side of (
I.23
) converges to zero for
H\searrow\frac{1}{2}
.

To show

\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\longrightarrow_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P})}\int_{0}^{T}g(X_{s}^{b})^{2}ds

as
H\searrow\frac{1}{2}
, we will prove that the family

\displaystyle\left(\left(\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\right)^{2}\right)_{\frac{1}{2}<H\leq\frac{3}{4}}

(I.26)

is uniformly integrable. Then it suffices to establish (cf.
[
27
]
, Theorem
5.12
)

\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}\int_{0}^{T}g(X_{s}^{b})^{2}ds.

By the reverse triangle inequality as in (
I.22
) and the reasoning thereafter, it is enough to show that
\|v_{H}^{g}-g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}
converges to zero in probability. For any
\epsilon>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\|v_{H}^{g}-g(X_{\cdot}^{b})\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}>\epsilon\right)\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(v_{H}^{g}(s)-g(X_{s}^{b})\right)^{2}ds\right]

by an application of Markov’s inequality and convergence of the right-hand side to zero for
H\searrow\frac{1}{2}
has been shown above, starting in (
I.23
). Consequently, it remains to prove uniform integrability of the family in (
I.26
). Without the summand
-g(X_{s}^{b})
inside the integral in the left-hand side of (
I.23
),

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\right]\leq 2A_{H}^{\prime}+2B_{H},

with

A_{H}^{\prime}=\|g\|_{\infty}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\frac{s^{\frac{1}{2}-H}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}\right)^{2}ds

and
B_{H}
just beyond (
I.23
).
Proceeding as above with
G_{H}
in (
I.25
) and
0<\epsilon_{H}<\min\{H-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}\}
therein,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq 2A_{H}^{\prime}+4B_{H}^{1}+8B_{H}^{2,1}+8B_{H}^{2,2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|g\|_{\infty}^{2}\left(\frac{T^{2-2H}}{(2-2H)\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)^{2}}-\frac{2T^{\frac{3}{2}-H}}{\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)}+T\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+4\frac{1}{(2-2H)\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)^{2}}\left(\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}-H\right)^{2}}{\Gamma(2-2H)}-1\right)^{2}\|g\|_{\infty}^{2}T^{2-2H}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+8(C^{\prime})^{2}\|g\|_{L}^{2}\frac{\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}}{\Gamma(3-2H)^{2}}\frac{1}{4-2H}T^{4-2H}(1+G_{H})^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+8\|g\|_{L}^{2}\sigma^{2}\frac{\left(H-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}}{\Gamma(2-H-\epsilon_{H})}\frac{1}{2-2\epsilon_{H}}T^{2-2\epsilon_{H}}G_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(g,T)\left(1+G_{H}^{2}\right)

for some constant
C(T,g)>0
depending only on
T
and
g
, where we used that uniformly over
H\in(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4}]
, the terms involving the Gamma function are bounded away from zero and infinity and the
T
-dependent terms are bounded. Hence, by Lemma
I.12
, for any integer
p>1
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\right)^{2p}\right]&\leq 2^{2p}C(g,T)^{2p}\mathbb{E}\left[1+G_{H}^{2p}\right]\\
&\leq 2^{2p}C(g,T)^{2p}\left(1+(32)^{2p}(2(T\vee 1))^{2p}\frac{(4p)!}{(2p)!}\right),\end{split}

(I.27)

where we used that
\epsilon_{H}
in the definition of
G_{H}
was chosen to be
\leq\frac{1}{4}
. The bound is independent of
H
and by Corollary
6.21
in
[
30
]
the family in (
I.26
) is uniformly integrable which completes the proof for
H>\frac{1}{2}
.
∎

Remember that in accordance with the fractional version of Girsanov’s theorem, Proposition
I.3
, we denote

Z^{1/2}(u_{s})=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{T}u_{s}dW_{s}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}u_{s}^{2}ds\right)

for the corresponding likelihood in the case
H=\frac{1}{2}
.

Proposition I.14
.

Let
b\in\Sigma(C,A,\gamma,\sigma)\cap\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)
,
L>0
. For any bounded and Lipschitz continuous function
g:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Z_{T}^{H}(g(X_{\cdot}^{H,b}))-Z_{T}^{1/2}(g(X_{\cdot}^{b}))\right|\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H\to\frac{1}{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

As
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
-convergence implies convergence in probability, we have by the continuous mapping theorem and Lemma
I.13
that

\displaystyle\exp\left(\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)dW_{s}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H\to\frac{1}{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}_{\mathbb{P}}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{T}g(X_{s}^{b})dW_{s}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}g(X_{s}^{b})^{2}ds\right)

for any bounded and Lipschitz continuous function
g
. We can conclude convergence in
L^{1}(\mathbb{P})
if the left-hand side is a uniformly integrable
H
-indexed family. We prove this by showing that its
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
-norm is uniformly bounded (see Corollary
6.21
in
[
30
]
), i.e.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(2\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)dW_{s}-\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\right)\right]\leq c

(I.28)

for some
c
independent of
H
.
As
v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}\geq 0
, it is enough to bound the expectation

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(2\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)dW_{s}\right)\right]

and we use monotone convergence and the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (cf.
[
27
]
, Theorem
20.12
) to bound the moments of the integral. The latter gives

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)dW_{s}\right|^{p}\right]\leq c_{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\right].

For the constant
c_{p}
we have
c_{p}\leq C^{\prime}p
for some constant
C^{\prime}>0
(see
[
6
]
, Proposition
4.2
).
Now we again distinguish the cases
H<\frac{1}{2}
and
H>\frac{1}{2}
. In the first one, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\right]\leq\|g\|_{\infty}^{p}T^{p},

which follows by the bound (
I.20
) established in the proof of Lemma
I.13
and hence by monotone convergence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(2\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)dW_{s}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|2\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)dW_{s}\right|^{k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{c_{k}2^{k}\|g\|_{\infty}^{k}T^{k}}{k!}

\displaystyle\leq 1+C^{\prime}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{(2\|g\|_{\infty}T)^{k}}{(k-1)!}

\displaystyle\leq 1+C^{\prime}(2\|g\|_{\infty}T)e^{2\|g\|_{\infty}T}<\infty.

In the case
H>\frac{1}{2}
we restrict to the interval
\frac{1}{2}<H<\frac{3}{4}
, which is possible as we consider convergence
H\searrow\frac{1}{2}
. First, as above,

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)dW_{s}\right)\right]\leq 1+C^{\prime}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{k2^{k}}{k!}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{k}{2}}\right].

Next, using that for any random variable
X\in L^{q}(\mathbb{P})
and
1\leq p\leq q
,

\mathbb{E}\left[|X|^{p}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|X|^{q}\right]^{p/q}\leq 1+\mathbb{E}\left[|X|^{q}\right],

and (
I.27
) subsequently,

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{k2^{k}}{k!}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{k}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 4C^{\prime}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{4k2^{4k}}{(4(k-1))!}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}v_{H}^{g}(s)^{2}ds\right)^{2k}\right]+1\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 4C^{\prime}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{4k2^{4k}}{(4(k-1))!}\left(2^{2k}C(T,g)^{2k}\left(1+(32)^{2k}(2(T\vee 1))^{2k}\frac{(4k)!}{(2k)!}\right)+1\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 4C^{\prime}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{(4k)^{5}}{(2k)!}\left(2^{5}C(g,T)32(T\vee 1)+2^{2}\right)^{2k}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 4^{6}C^{\prime}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{k^{5}}{k!}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\left(2^{5}C(g,T)32(T\vee 1)+2^{2}\right)^{2}\right)^{k},

where convergence of the series follows by a comparison with the exponential series as
k^{5}/k!
is bounded.

In consequence, (
I.28
) holds for both cases
H<\frac{1}{2}
and
H>\frac{1}{2}
, which establishes the
L^{1}(\mathbb{P})
-convergence of the Girsanov densities for a bounded and Lipschitz continuous function
g:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
.
∎

As in the proof of Theorem
5.1
, we bound

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{\psi_{T}^{H}}\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in H_{1}(b_{0},\eta)\cap\{b-b_{0}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)\}:\\
\Delta_{J}(b)\geq(1-\epsilon_{T})c_{*}\delta_{T}\end{subarray}}\ \mathbb{E}\left[\psi_{T}^{H}(X^{H,b})\right]-\alpha\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{N_{T}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{T}}Z_{T}^{H}\left((b_{k}-b_{0,\eta})(X^{H,b_{0,\eta}})\right)-1\right)\phi_{T}^{H}\right]\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{N_{T}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{T}}Z_{T}^{H}\left((b_{k}-b_{0,\eta})(X^{H,b_{0,\eta}})\right)-1\right|\right]\end{split}

(I.29)

and in particular choose the same hypothesis
b_{k}
as in this proof, which are bounded by one and Lipschitz continuous. Now, by adding zero, this term is again bounded by the sum of

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{N_{T}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{T}}Z_{T}^{1/2}\left((b_{k}-b_{0,\eta})(X^{b_{0,\eta}})\right)-1\right|\right]

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{N_{T}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{T}}\left(Z_{T}^{H}\left((b_{k}-b_{0,\eta})(X^{H,b_{0,\eta}})\right)-Z_{T}^{1/2}\left((b_{k}-b_{0,\eta})(X^{b_{0,\eta}})\right)\right)\right|\right]\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\frac{1}{N_{T}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{T}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Z_{T}^{H}\left((b_{k}-b_{0,\eta})(X^{H,b_{0,\eta}})\right)-Z_{T}^{1/2}\left((b_{k}-b_{0,\eta})(X^{b_{0,\eta}})\right)\right|\right].\end{split}

(I.30)

Let
\epsilon>0
. Then, by the proof of Theorem
5.1
in combination with Remark
5.5
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{T\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{N_{T}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{T}}Z_{T}^{1/2}\left((b_{k}-b_{0,\eta})(X^{b_{0,\eta}})\right)-1\right|\right]<\frac{\epsilon}{2},

(I.31)

By Proposition
I.14
there exists
\delta_{k}(T,\epsilon)>0
such that for
|H-\frac{1}{2}|<\delta_{k}(T,\epsilon)
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Z_{T}^{H}\left((b_{k}-b_{0,\eta})(X^{H,b_{0,\eta}})\right)-Z_{T}^{1/2}\left((b_{k}-b_{0,\eta})(X^{b_{0,\eta}})\right)\right|\right]<\frac{\epsilon}{2}

for every
k=1,\dots,N_{T}
.
Define
\delta(T):=\delta(T,\epsilon):=\min_{1\leq k\leq N}\delta_{k}(T,\epsilon)>0
. Then,

\sup_{|H-\frac{1}{2}|<\delta(T)}\max_{1\leq k\leq N_{T}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Z_{T}^{H}\hskip-1.42271pt\left((b_{k}-b_{0,\eta})(X^{H,b_{0,\eta}})\right)\hskip-1.42271pt-\hskip-1.42271ptZ_{T}^{1/2}\hskip-1.42271pt\left((b_{k}-b_{0,\eta})(X^{b_{0,\eta}})\right)\right|\right]

is bounded from above by
\frac{\epsilon}{2}
. In particular,
\sup_{|H-\frac{1}{2}|<\delta(T)}
of the right-hand side of (
I.30
) is bounded from above by
\frac{\epsilon}{2}
and the claim of the theorem follows with (
I.29
) and (
I.31
).
∎
